# S2E03 "EPIPHANY" — CONTEXT PACK (writers' room only; contains 🔒 hidden canon)

**Built by the Context Packer, 2026-10-23; UPDATED in place (update pass: 1.0b player notes + brief L1552-L1658 appended to 1B); for the Fri Jan 1 – Mon Jan 11, 2010 episode (revision 2 of `writers/outlines/s2e03.md`).**
Every later agent reads this INSTEAD of the 1 MB bible, the 155 KB brief, the 500 KB continuity ledger and the 189 KB knowledge ledger, and consults those only
by targeted lookup. Every excerpt below is **verbatim** and carries a `[SRC file · heading · line range]` label so it can be checked at the source (line numbers are
those of the files as of this build). Nothing here is paraphrased except the short bracketed `[Packer's note]`s and section intros.

**How to read the 🔒 marks.** 🔒 = hidden canon (the player must never learn it from the page). It may constrain, color or be planted in an episode; it may NEVER be
surfaced in `journal`, `cue`, `key`, `previously`, `logline`, `nextTime`, choice text, a card, or any narration that states another character's inner state as fact (POV: Jack only).
Markdown headings that occur INSIDE excerpts are backslash-escaped (`\## The show`) so that this pack's own numbered outline stays navigable; apart from that backslash the excerpts are byte-for-byte the source.

Where the brief and the bible conflict, **the brief wins**; where a newer brief entry and an older one conflict, **the newest wins** (entries are in the brief's own order, oldest to newest;
the newest entries are the last ones in Section 1B).

**Authority order for this episode:** (1) the HARD RULES and content standards (Section 1A); (2) the brief's newest entries (Section 1B, last blocks first) and the player notes (Section 1.0);
(3) the published `episodes/s2e02.json` and `saves/after-s2e01.json` as summarized by the S2E02 Head Writer handoff (Section 1D) and the continuity ledger (Section 6); (4) the outline `outlines/s2e03.md` rev. 2
(the showrunner plan — read it in full alongside this pack: its §0 binding notes, §3 beats and §9 gates are NOT duplicated here; its §0 "may not" list, §2 seeds, §6a roster, §9.4, §10 LIVE RISKS and §11 tic caps ARE quoted below); (5) the bible.

## 1A. Hard rules, content standards, mechanics (brief front matter — verbatim)


**[SRC brief.md · The show (format, era, tone, faith lens) · L1–21]**

\# THE HARD SAYING — Creative Brief (binding)

This is the player's brief, assembled from a long design interview. Everything here is a decision the player made. It is
NOT secret: the player wrote it. Hidden canon lives in `bible.md`. Where the bible and this brief conflict, the brief wins.

\## The show
- **Format:** A text-based, choice-driven game presented as a prestige-television college drama, played in episodes and
  seasons. Each episode is a full TV hour: cold open, acts, tag. **12,000–18,000 words**, 15–25 meaningful choices.
- **Title:** *The Hard Saying* (John 6:60: "This is a hard saying; who can listen to it?").
- **Structure:** 1 season = 1 semester, **22 episodes**, plus an abbreviated **summer season** between school years.
  The run covers four years of college and continues into adulthood after graduation.
- **Era:** Starts **Fall 2009** (move-in late August 2009) and moves forward through real history. Real events, politics,
  people in the news, pop culture, and Church events (Benedict XVI, *Caritas in Veritate*, the health-care fight, the
  recession, H1N1, Facebook, flip phones and T9, later the 2012 election, Benedict's resignation and Francis's election
  in 2013) stay in the background and shape conversations. Get them right; the liturgical calendar is real.
- **Influences:** Hart of Dixie, Gilmore Girls, Greek, Euphoria, Entourage, Task, Red Dead Redemption 2, The Witcher 3,
  The Last of Us, the Cormoran Strike novels.
- **Tone:** *Warmth first, knife second.* A blend of sweetness, warmth, comedy, heat, tension, darkness, grief and pain.
  Mostly Gilmore/Hart of Dixie warmth and banter so that the darkness lands harder when it comes.
- **Faith lens:** *True, and hard.* The story treats Catholicism as true and serious: grace is real, sin has weight. The
  faith is never easy, and its critics get their best arguments. Flannery O'Connor, not a youth-group pamphlet.


**[SRC brief.md · The protagonist: Jack Costigan — POV and narration · L23–33]**

\## The protagonist: Jack Costigan (the player)
- Freshman (18) from **Youngstown, Ohio**: Rust Belt Irish/Italian, working-class, parish school, first in his family at
  a big university. Devout Catholic. Philosophy major.
- **Charming, restless, reckless.** Magnetic, funny, a little dangerous. His faith is real, and so is the pull of the
  world. Arthur Morgan-adjacent: a good man who may not believe he is.
- Well-read, bright, loves philosophy and theology. Argues for sport and for keeps.
- **POV: Jack only.** The player is Jack. Lena stays a mystery read through her words and actions. Never show a scene
  Jack isn't in. Never narrate another character's thoughts as fact.
- **Narration: close third person, present tense.** ("Jack watches her cross the porch.")
- His family appears on-screen: phone calls, holidays, trouble at home.


**[SRC brief.md · The love interest: Lena Broussard · L34–41]**

\## The love interest: Magdalena "Lena" Broussard
- Freshman (18) from **New Orleans**. Southern Catholic culture: big family, crawfish boils, Mardi Gras and Lent, faith
  as heritage and food and noise. Classics/philosophy student, so she is **Jack's direct rival** in the same seminars,
  competing for the same mentor's approval.
- **Fierce and incandescent.** Quick, combative, funny, impossible to out-talk. Loves a fight and wins most of them.
  Underneath is a tenderness she guards ferociously. Lorelai crossed with Catherine of Siena. Beautiful and magnetic.
- The Broussards appear on-screen: Thanksgiving and Mardi Gras episodes; loud, close, complicated.


**[SRC brief.md · Looks (the player chose these) — all principals · L97–121]**

\## Looks (the player chose these)
- **Lena:** sun-streaked honey-brown waves, golden skin, warm amber-brown eyes; classic Gulf Coast beauty. About 5'5",
  soft and curvy, at peace with her body, neither hiding nor flaunting it; warmth in every line. Wears her
  grandmother's worn gold **Miraculous Medal** on a thin chain and touches it when she's thinking or lying. Thrifted
  **sundresses with beat-up cowboy boots** or Keds, a cardigan when the Marisol fog rolls in. Sometimes veils at Mass
  in a **black lace mantilla**, which draws looks even at Newman.
- **Jack:** 6'0", black hair that needs cutting, pale skin, blue eyes, a nose broken once in a CYO basketball fight,
  stubble by noon. Wears a **St. Michael medal** from his confirmation, and has a **scar through one eyebrow** whose
  story he tells differently every time. Always a battered **paperback in his back pocket** (Chesterton, Camus,
  Percy), spine cracked, margins full. Smokes **Marlboro Reds** when stressed or drinking, and is "quitting"; it's a
  whole thing with Lena.
- **Theo:** golden LA prince. Tall, tanned, artfully messy blond hair, green eyes, very expensive clothes worn like
  they're cheap. Looks like a Calvin Klein ad and knows it.
- **Laney:** redhead bombshell. Copper hair, porcelain skin, freckles she covers, a laugh you can hear across a party.
  Petite, all hips and attitude.
- **Margot:** severe Scandinavian. White-blonde hair cut blunt at the jaw, very pale, thick black-framed glasses, all
  black clothing, Doc Martens. Seems to have no body temperature.
- **Kevin:** gym-built pre-med. Compact and muscular, a crew cut, an athletic quarter-zip, a big easy smile that never
  quite reaches his eyes.
- **Fr. Osei:** gentle and weathered. Medium height, thin, a graying beard, deep laugh lines, a slight limp he never explains.
- **Prof. Hale:** rumpled Oxford rake. Tall, silver hair swept back too long, a linen jacket with wine on the cuff, a
  once-beautiful face gone ruddy and broken-veined. Still devastating when he smiles.
- **Julian:** quietly handsome. Medium height, clean-cut, warm brown skin, short dark hair, a calm presence; you'd
  trust him with anything. Musician.


**[SRC brief.md · Mechanics (meters, soul track, traits, whispers, resolve); Aesthetic (period texture, needle-drops) · L143–169]**

\## Mechanics
- **Hidden relationship meters** per core character: trust, warmth, heat, friction. Never shown as numbers; the player
  feels them in how people talk to Jack.
- **Soul track:** grace vs. drift, honesty vs. performance, restraint vs. abandon. It shapes Jack's inner voice and which
  options are available to him.
- **Jack's traits** firm up through choices (wit, sincerity, charm, candor, recklessness, control) and unlock unique
  dialogue.
- **Journal / clue board**, visible, Strike-style: people, clues, notes, and the story so far.
- **Choice types:** dialogue tone and content; where to go and who to be with (you can't be everywhere); **argument
  duels**, a philosophy-fight mini-game with Lena (pick premises, concede or press; rigor, wit and heart score; winning
  isn't always good for the relationship).
- **Feedback:** Telltale whispers ("Lena will remember that."), used sparingly.
- **Resolve:** hidden resolve for Jack and for Lena, rising and falling with prayer, confession, stress, alcohol and
  loneliness. In charged scenes, the player's choices act on Jack's resolve; Lena's is hidden and set by the writers.
  When both run low, the dangerous night becomes possible.
- **Rewind:** the player may restart the *current* episode from its beginning before finishing it. Once finished, the
  episode is locked forever.
- **Notes session** before every episode (Claude runs it with AskUserQuestion). Honor the notes without spoiling secrets.

\## Aesthetic
- **2009 period texture:** prestige-TV base (title cards, "Previously on…", chyron scene headers like
  "THURSDAY · 11:47 PM · DELGADO HALL, 4TH FLOOR") plus period artifacts as gameplay inserts: Facebook wall posts,
  T9 texts on a flip phone, AIM away messages, an iPod.
- **Text needle-drops:** real songs, era-accurate (released by the scene's date), cued at key moments like a TV music
  supervisor would.
- Dark theme by default, with a light toggle.


**[SRC brief.md · Content standards (explicitness; everyone 18+; sexual assault; HARD LINES) · L170–183]**

\## Content standards
- **Explicitness:** the player asked for the narrative to be as graphic, explicit and unflinching as permitted, always in
  service of character, story and emotion. In practice: sex, desire, bodies, drug use, violence, addiction, overdose,
  death and grief are written frankly, with real sensory and physical detail, at the level of literary fiction or
  HBO drama. Sex scenes can be on the page and charged, but they are written as literature (emotion, sensation, the
  specific people), not pornographic mechanics. The ensemble's sex lives are shown frankly (Euphoria-honest): the fun,
  the morning after, the loneliness. Neither preachy nor glamorized.
- **Everyone in any sexual content is an adult (18+).** All freshmen are 18 or older; say so in the bible.
- **Sexual assault** may be a storyline, handled seriously and centered on the person's experience and aftermath.
  Never titillating, never a cheap twist.
- Addiction and overdose (someone may not make it), death and grief, crime and violence (a mystery thread) are all allowed.
- **Hard lines:** the narrative never mocks the faith (critics can be sharp and right; the story never sneers). No cheap
  villains or secular strawmen; everyone has their reasons. **Jack and Lena never die.**


**[SRC room.md · Knowledge Auditor checklist (the "knowledge tics" rules) — also reproduced in Section 7 · L29–40]**

\## Knowledge Auditor checklist
For every line of dialogue or narration that implies knowledge, ask: **how does this character know this?**
- A character may only reference what they witnessed, were told on-page, or could plausibly learn off-page (and if it's
  off-page, the text must make the route clear: "Laney told me").
- **Banned move:** a character says something they can't know, then plays it off as a joke, guess or intuition
  ("Lucky guess." "I'm psychic." "Call it a hunch."). Cut it or give them a real route.
- Jack's narration knows only what Jack knows. No narrating others' inner states as fact ("she's thinking about…").
  Use observation and inference: "something in her face says…".
- Names: a character doesn't use a name before it's been introduced to them. Jack doesn't know Lena's name until she
  gives it (or someone says it in his hearing).
- Track it in `knowledge.md`: per character, the major facts they know and the episode/scene where they learned it.

**[SRC brief.md · Faith tests: bent, never broken; Cadence: the player binges · L212–215]**

- **Faith tests: bent, never broken.** Grief, scandal and argument shake both of them, but neither ever truly stops
  believing. The tests are about *living* it (fidelity, temptation, anger at God, the Church's failures), not whether it's
  true.
- **Cadence: the player binges.** Keep "Previously on" light (4–6 lines) and callbacks dense. Trust the player's memory.


**[SRC brief.md · After S1E04 — standing rule: an extended Jack/Lena two-hander roughly every other episode · L252–254]**

- **After S1E04 (player feedback).** "Little ache, lot of tenderness, more ache. The Lena-Jack extended two-person scene
  was fantastic." Standing rule: **an extended Jack/Lena two-hander roughly every other episode** (E06, E08, …; the
  room may shift by one when the story demands). For E05: tone and mix per the room's plan; no other notes.


**[SRC brief.md · Explicitness, refined (POV stays Jack-only) · L263–266]**

- **Explicitness, refined.** "As set, dialed up": frank, literary, on the page when the story calls for it, a notch
  more than so far. **POV stays Jack-only** (confirmed: no other POV has been used). Never cut to another character's
  POV just for a sex scene. The ensemble's sex lives appear as Jack witnesses, hears, or lives the before and after,
  and in Jack's own scenes when they come.


**[SRC brief.md · Jack's two girls: player's design (binding) · L270–286]**

- **Jack's two girls: player's design (binding).**
  - **The tryer** ("the girl you bring home to mama"): cute, sweet, warm, great in so many ways. She genuinely tries
    with his faith *and* his intellectual rigor. But she doesn't have the allure over him that Lena and the magnetic
    girl do: it's nice, it's warm, and there's nothing deeper there. Likely Catholic too, either less devout, or devout
    but without depth. The ending is sad because she did everything right. **Laney hates her on principle; Lena tells
    Laney to be nice; Theo is always polite but always Team Lena.**
  - **The magnetic girl**: her flaws sit exactly where the tryer's strengths are. She's respectful of his faith and
    mildly curious, but not really interested in it. She's fierce, intelligent, funny, sexy and utterly compelling: a
    real temptation that truly draws Jack in (resolve and restraint are tested). **Laney is openly rude to her like all the others, but privately, begrudgingly likes her; Lena
    can't stand her; Theo knows her (from before or from his world) and, as always, remains Team Lena.**
  - Design principle: the tryer has the faith and the effort without the fire; the magnetic girl has the fire without
    the faith. Only Lena has both. Neither girl is a strawman, and each must be someone the player could love.
  - **Refinements:** the tryer is a **sincere, shallow cradle Catholic**. She goes to Mass, prays her grandmother's
    rosary and loves the community, but has never wrestled with any of it; Jack's arguments delight her and slide off.
    **Jack meets her at the Newman Center**, the nicest girl there; everyone thinks they're perfect together. The
    magnetic girl comes from **Theo's LA orbit** (an actress, a director's daughter or similar: sharp, glamorous), and
    Theo knows exactly what she is. **Which girl comes first** (in the timing-tragedy slot): room decides.


**[SRC brief.md · Lena's romantic interests; the secret alliance (player's exact design); the alliance reveal; the proxy fights; HARD RULE (neither Jack nor Lena ever sleeps with anyone else) · L312–353]**

- **Lena's romantic interests (player directives).**
  - **Julian's fatal gap: both.** He respects her faith without ever believing it (he stands lovingly outside the
    church door; no sacramental marriage is possible), *and* he's too easy: gentle and deferential, he never fights her,
    and she realizes she needs to be argued with. Perfect on paper and wrong in the bones; the ending is sad.
  - **Lena's temptation: room builds him.** He mirrors Jack's magnetic girl: someone with real pull (charming,
    dangerous or brilliant) whose flaws sit where Julian's strengths are, indifferent to her faith. Jack has to watch.
  - **On screen through Jack's eyes:** couples at parties, hands held at Mass, a door closing. Jealousy as POV.
  - **The friends: the secret alliance (player's exact design; supersedes earlier notes).** Theo and Laney run a
    covert pro-Jack-and-Lena alliance, and they mirror each other:
    - **On Jack's girlfriends and dates:** **Laney openly hates them and is rude** to every one of them. **Lena tells
      her to stop and be nice** to all of them *except the magnetic girl*, whom Lena can't stand either. **Theo is
      perfectly polite in the open** but privately Team Lena: he passes information to Laney for planning, and they
      maneuver together.
    - **On Lena's boyfriends and dates (after Wes):** **Theo openly hates them and is rude** to every one of them, and
      **Jack mostly doesn't stop him**. With **Julian**, Jack eventually tells Theo to stop: he can tell it isn't going
      to work out, and it's turning mean for no reason. With **Lena's magnetic guy**, Jack hates him too and quietly
      thanks Theo for it. **Laney is perfectly polite in the open** but privately Team Jack: she passes information to
      Theo for planning, and they maneuver together.
    - **Margot** likes Julian and tells Jack he's the healthiest thing in Lena's life.
    - **Kevin** tries to get Theo and Laney to leave it alone; both refuse.
- **The alliance reveal (reserved payoff).** In the **first ensemble set-piece episode after Jack and Lena finally get
  together**, Theo and Laney openly reveal their secret alliance, toasting themselves with champagne for pulling it
  off, and discover **everyone already knew**, Jack and Lena included. The beat (the player's lines; keep them):
  "We know." / "Everyone knows." / "People outside of California know." / "It was obvious after five minutes."
  Until then, the alliance's maneuvering should be visible to the player and to the ensemble but never acknowledged
  aloud, so the reveal pays off years of comic evidence.
- **The proxy fights (reserved, recurring).** While either magnetic relationship or attraction is going on (Jack's
  magnetic girl, Lena's magnetic guy), Jack and Lena have a **serious fight with each other** that is **100% about the
  other person being with someone else, and neither can say it**. If the two periods don't overlap, it happens at least
  once in each. The fight is ostensibly about something else: a philosophy point, a slight, politics, a friend, a
  forgotten promise, how the other is "changing." It is vicious, specific and personal, and the subtext is obvious to
  the player (and to Theo, Laney and Margot), but the real reason is **never spoken** aloud by either of them during
  that period. These can play as argument duels where "winning" the stated argument is the wrong thing to win.
  Payoff: later (likely after they're together), one of them finally names what those fights were really about.
- **HARD RULE: neither Jack nor Lena ever sleeps with anyone else.** Not with Wes, Julian, the tryer, the magnetic girl,
  the magnetic guy, or anyone. Sleeping with someone else would undermine the entire premarital-temptation arc when they
  are finally together: their first time (whenever and however the player's choices make it happen) is with each
  other. Kissing, making out, real physical temptation and near-misses with others are allowed and can be charged; the
  line holds short of sex, and the resolve system should make it cost something. No backstory sex either: neither has
  slept with anyone before the series. **No sexual assault or rape in either of their backgrounds** (or happening to
  them during the series). Other serious wounds are fine: family rupture, grief, violence, addiction in the family,
  crime, illness, emotional or other non-sexual harm from a past relationship, faith crises, and so on.


**[SRC brief.md · What's working (S1E06 chastity talk) — the Ephesians-avoidance running bit · L358–363]**

- **What's working (S1E06 chastity talk).** The player loved the Newman chastity-talk scene "every bit of it": the
  tongue-in-cheek humor, and **taking the concept seriously while not taking the approach seriously** (Lena's aside to
  Jack summed it up). Their **mutual agreement not to discuss marital submission** got a big laugh. Keep it as a running
  bit: they visibly, comically keep avoiding Ephesians 5 until the reserved conversation finally arrives (see "Marital
  submission"). This register (sincere about the faith, satirical about Catholic-subculture packaging) is a model for
  faith scenes going forward.


**[SRC brief.md · Kevin's arc; Deaths (the core eight survive) · L364–372]**

- **Kevin's arc (player directive, long-range).** A **long struggle** whose outcome is shaped heavily by his friendship
  with Jack and Lena. Lots of back and forth: brief departures from the Church, probably a same-sex relationship or two,
  other lapses, maybe even a sad attempt at a relationship with a girl, before **he finally settles as a celibate gay
  Catholic**. Write it with compassion and full cost, no easy answers, and no character a mouthpiece. **Margot** is
  frequently offended on his behalf and disagrees loudly with Jack, Lena and Kevin over the Church's teaching, but she
  comes to accept and respect that Jack and Lena genuinely love him.
- **Deaths.** **The core eight survive the college years** (Jack, Lena, Theo, Laney, Margot, Kevin, Fr. Osei,
  Prof. Hale). After college a real, earned loss is allowed if the story calls for it (by then the cast around Jack and
  Lena may be quite different). Losses during college may fall on family, guests and the wider cast.


**[SRC brief.md · After S1E07 — Becca: the guilt · L378–381]**

- **After S1E07 (player feedback).** "Such a good episode!" Lena the standout. Becca was sweet and **the player felt
  guilty the entire time through Jack** (exactly right: keep that guilt alive while Becca's in the picture). Loved the
  twist of heartache at the end with the paper, and **the little knife of Lena telling Jack to eat the maple bar Becca
  saved for him** (small, generous, devastating gestures from Lena land hard; use them sparingly).


**[SRC brief.md · Running bits: never ask Laney; the alliance's false dawns; later Jack/Lena conversation; availability windows; Theo's darkness; Laney · L399–427]**

- **Running bit: never ask Laney for girl advice.** At some point (this third date, a later date with Becca, or a date
  or gesture for the magnetic girl; room's choice of when and for whom), Jack asks Laney for an idea for a date or
  gesture. Her answer is **hilariously terrible**, and it is **deliberately sabotage** (secret-alliance work). Laney
  confirms this later, ideally at or near the alliance reveal. After that, **Jack never asks Laney for girl advice
  again**, and it becomes a callback.
- **Running bit: the alliance's false dawns.** Every time Jack or Lena gets out of a relationship, **Theo and Laney are
  absolutely pumped** (celebrations, plans, maybe premature champagne) and then **deeply bummed** when they realize the
  other one *isn't* immediately breaking up with whoever they're with to chase after them. It escalates each time and
  pairs with the timing tragedy. The first instance is likely when Lena and Wes end while Jack is with someone. Pays
  off in the champagne at the alliance reveal.
- **Later Jack/Lena conversation (while Jack is still with Becca; after E08).** Jack tells Lena that Becca asked him to
  explain the first of Aquinas's Five Ways (the argument from motion) **during a football game**. "Aquinas and football,
  that's the dream girl right there," Jack says, **not realizing until later he has just described Lena**. Lena
  notices, **without comment** (Jack sees something cross her face, or realizes it himself hours later). Then he says it
  was just empty: Becca was relentlessly happy he'd told her, with no engagement with it at all. This is the stage
  where **Jack knows he shouldn't be with Becca** (the progression: E08 "she's so nice and sweet" → this → the end).
  Fit to their voices, not verbatim.
- **Structural rule: availability windows.** Whenever Jack or Lena leaves a relationship while the other is free, they
  would realistically just get together; neither could start something new with someone else while the other is
  openly available. So **every breakup with a "nice" partner (Becca, Julian, Wes) must coincide with a real reason
  Jack and Lena don't get together right then**: most often the other is now in a relationship (e.g., Jack ends things
  with Becca just as Lena starts with Julian or someone new), or another concrete block (distance, summer, a fight).
  **The magnetic periods are the exception:** with Jack's magnetic girl or Lena's magnetic guy, one can genuinely be
  *angry* at the other for a while, and that anger (plus the proxy fights) can keep them apart even when both are
  technically free. Plan every breakup's timing against this rule; it drives the timing tragedies and the alliance's
  false dawns.
- **Theo's darkness: mostly hidden.** It stays under the charm, glimpsed rather than shown (the 5:20 a.m. returns, the
  forgiveness question, the Point), until a late reveal. No public collapse.
- **Laney: mostly fun.** The pills and rush darkness stays an undertow; she is the comic engine. No overdose scare.


**[SRC brief.md · The hurt chain (player directive; the emotional engine of the re-plan) · L547–559]**

- **The hurt chain (player directive; the emotional engine of the re-plan).** **Clarified by the player: Jack and Lena
  stay just as close as they've been, and keep getting closer and more honest.** No distance in the friendship, no
  reduced time together, no cooling. The *hurt* each one's relationship choices cause the other is what makes it
  believable that they don't simply get together: each keeps being wounded, silently, by the other's choices, and acts
  out of that hurt (staying, starting something new), even as the friendship deepens. The sequence:
  1. **Lena is single (Wes ends) and Jack doesn't break up with Becca.** Lena is **hurt but says nothing** (Jack sees
     only traces). That hurt **leads her to Julian**.
  2. **Jack is hurt that she didn't wait.** That hurt feeds **his Christmas-break conversations with the magnetic girl**
     (Facebook, texts). **He still talks to Lena as much as ever** over the break; both threads run at once.
  3. **When Lena finds out about the magnetic girl** (early S2), she's **hurt that *he* didn't wait** and
     **increasingly mad at Jack about her**, and she **won't end it with Julian even though she knows it's a dead end**.
  Neither ever says the hurt aloud until New Orleans. It comes out sideways (proxy fights, coolness with others,
  overcorrections) while the friendship itself stays close.


**[SRC brief.md · Why Jack stays with Becca; Tone of the stretch (a proxy war); The New Orleans conversation must include this · L566–588]**

- **Why Jack stays with Becca when Lena is single (player's choice): he won't be the rebound.** Not cowardice. Lena's
  breakup is fresh and painful (and Wes had a ring), and Jack refuses to swoop in on her grief: she deserves time, and
  he won't be the guy waiting at the door. **He means to wait, decently**, and to end things with Becca honestly in due
  course. **Lena reads it as him choosing Becca.** That misreading is the first link of the hurt chain (she goes to
  Julian). Jack's reason should be legible to the player through his narration and choices, and genuinely admirable
  and genuinely wrong at once. When Lena starts with Julian, the waiting turns into Jack's own hurt ("she didn't wait").
  It all comes out in New Orleans: he was waiting *for* her; she thought he'd chosen.
- **Tone of the stretch (Nov 2009 – Feb 2010): a proxy war.** The next several months of their separate relationships
  are **functionally a proxy war between a couple that hasn't gotten together yet**, played as **darkly funny while
  sad**. Each new relationship move reads (to the player, the alliance, and eventually them) as a volley at the other:
  dates scheduled where the other will see, Julian and the magnetic girl unknowingly drafted as combatants, competitive
  happiness on Facebook, escalating politeness, holiday-gift arms races. Theo and Laney run their mirrored covert ops
  in the middle of it, and Margot and Kevin comment from the sidelines. The partners (Becca, Julian, the magnetic girl)
  are never punchlines; the comedy is in Jack and Lena. The ache underneath is real: they're closer and more honest
  with each other than with the people they're with.
- **The New Orleans conversation must include this (player's design; fully honest from both).** Jack explains that he
  didn't want to hurt Becca, **but the real reason he didn't come for Lena after she and Wes broke up** was that he
  didn't want to swoop in while she was hurting from something that big, **and he didn't think he'd be able to keep
  himself from doing it if he were single too.** Staying with Becca was a guardrail against himself. Lena answers
  with equal honesty: how she read it (that he'd chosen Becca), the hurt, why Julian, and why she couldn't leave Julian
  once the magnetic girl appeared. Jack owns his side too (the magnetic girl, his hurt that she didn't wait). The proxy
  war gets named and laughed at, painfully. This is the payoff of the misreading chain, said without letting go of each
  other, before Lena pulls away. (Fit to their voices; not verbatim.)


**[SRC brief.md · Laney's first sighting (player's line; keep verbatim) — S2E04's, UNSPENT · L645–648]**

  - **Laney's first sighting (player's line; keep verbatim).** The first time Laney sees Skye and Jack together (the
    sun-streaked hair and the fire read as a copy of Lena), she says to Lena: **"Oh my God, he found a California you.
    Excuse me, while I go be a bitch on your behalf."** Then she goes and does it (alliance rules: Laney openly rude to
    Jack's girls). Lena's reaction is the knife: she hates that Laney's right.


**[SRC brief.md · The Costigans on Skye (player directive) · L709–715]**

- **The Costigans on Skye (player directive).** They never meet her (no chance between January and the NOLA trip), but
  when Jack's family finds out about her, **especially once they see her Facebook and who she is** (the TV show, the
  photos), **they're all the way out on Skye**: Maria, Frank, Nonna, Gina, each in their own register (Nonna by rosary
  and dark Italian, Gina mortified and thrilled about the famous part, Maria and Frank worried about "that kind of
  girl," and all of them asking about Lena). **Except Danny, who thinks it's hilarious and that Jack must have tricked
  her somehow.** (Danny is in prison with no Facebook: he hears about it on the phone or in letters, maybe from Gina,
  who describes the photos in detail.)


**[SRC brief.md · ACCELERATED SEQUENCE steps 8–9; Accelerated sequence, details · L732–748]**

  8. **They decide to be dating when they get back after the break.** In Skye's voice: not before, because Christmas
     and New Year's put too much pressure on new things. Over Christmas break in Youngstown they keep texting and
     Facebooking (and the Costigans find her Facebook; see "The Costigans on Skye").
  9. January 2010: officially dating; Lena finds out (the hurt chain's third link) and stays with Julian; proxy war;
     the fundraiser; New Orleans in February.
  (This aligns Jack's timeline earlier: Becca ends pre-Thanksgiving rather than right after it. The "lie bordering on
  cruel" Thanksgiving realization now happens *before* Thanksgiving, triggered by Julian.)
- **Accelerated sequence, details (player).**
  - **Thanksgiving:** Jack had been planning to ask Lena home, but **doesn't once she tells him about Julian**. **Theo
    comes home to Youngstown with him.** Theo comments that Jack is texting a lot; Jack says nothing.
  - **Theo and Laney:** crushed by Lena's new boyfriend (a false dawn), then **in a frenzy that both Jack and Lena are
    dating other people again**. Both are **suspicious of Jack for the rest of the semester** (his unexplained absences
    from Thanksgiving to Christmas).
  - **Christmas break:** **Theo runs into Skye** (in LA). **Skye tells Jack** (Facebook message, text or phone call)
    that his roommate was being extremely odd, even for him. **The larger part of that conversation is how Jack learns
    that Theo knows Skye** (childhood friends) **and suspects them.** The meeting confirmed Theo's suspicions: **Theo
    and Laney return from Christmas break well-prepared** for their planning (alliance ops ready for Season 2).


**[SRC brief.md · Open questions resolved (player) — Lena's reaction; Jack and Skye's first conversation; How Lena learns about Skye (January 2010); overheard fragments; Christmas-break calls · L749–777]**

- **Open questions resolved (player).**
  - **Lena's reaction when Jack ends it with Becca (pre-Thanksgiving): quiet devastation.** She says the right
    supportive things, then goes back to her room. Jack sees nothing; Laney sees everything. It confirms her misreading:
    he waited until she was taken.
  - **Jack and Skye's first conversation: a book.** She's reading something for a role (e.g., Flannery O'Connor or
    Dostoevsky) and has it wrong. He corrects her without knowing who she is, and she loves that he doesn't care.
  - **Lena and Julian started simply: he asked, gently, the week after Wes, and she said yes.**
  - **How Lena learns about Skye (January 2010):** Lena had **privately resolved over Christmas to break up with Julian**
    because there was nothing there. Then **she sees the Facebook status** ("In a Relationship with Skye Callender")
    and keeps veering between devastated, furious and sick. **Laney physically takes the laptop away**, then looks
    through the pictures and interactions herself **and gets mad too**. Lena does NOT break up with Julian (the hurt
    chain).
    - **POV rule:** this happens off Jack's screen. Jack learns pieces later (Laney's cold fury, Lena's sudden
      formality, Theo's report); the **full story, including that Lena had decided to end it with Julian over Christmas,
      comes out in the New Orleans conversation**, where it lands as the cruelest near-miss of the whole misreading
      chain.
    - **Overheard fragments (player):** in January–February 2010, Jack also **catches fragments of Theo and Laney
      talking about it** that **don't make sense out of context** ("…she'd *decided*, Theo…", "…over Christmas…", "…the
      laptop's in my closet…", "…don't you dare tell him…"). They stop or change the subject when he walks in. Plant
      them so they click into place in New Orleans when Lena tells him she'd decided to leave Julian. The Knowledge
      Auditor must ensure Jack never infers the full meaning early.
    - **Christmas break calls (player):** Jack and Lena **talk constantly over Christmas break** (Youngstown ↔ New
      Orleans: long late-night phone calls, Facebook messages, texts at midnight Mass), and **it feels really meaningful
      to both**. To Lena, it's part of why she decides to leave Julian. To Jack it is just as meaningful, but she's with
      Julian, so he reads it as friendship he mustn't push, all while he's also texting Skye. **When Lena sees the
      Skye status in January, the betrayal is sharpened by those calls**: he was talking to her like *that* all break
      while starting something with someone else. Play the calls as warm and charged on Jack's side of the screen (the
      player should feel how much they mean); the dramatic irony is that neither says what they mean. Payoff in New
      Orleans.


**[SRC brief.md · A proxy fight beat (player's design) · L778–783]**

- **A proxy fight beat (player's design; Lena's voice, not verbatim).** During one of their sideways fights in the
  Skye period, in the middle of a larger conflict about something else, Lena yells some version of: "If we're such
  'good friends,' how come I had to find out about your girlfriend from a Facebook status when we talked every single
  day for weeks leading up to it?" It's the closest the proxy war comes to the truth before New Orleans: she says the
  grievance, never the reason. Jack has no good answer (he never told her because telling her would have meant saying
  why it mattered). The fight then veers back to its ostensible subject, and neither names the real one.


**[SRC brief.md · Length exception for the Skye episodes · L790–794]**

- **Length exception for the Skye episodes (player).** The episodes that carry Jack and Skye (from their meeting
  through Season 2's New Orleans trip) **may run longer than the 12–18k range, up to about 22k words per path**, so
  there is plenty of Jack-and-Skye on the page **without losing the ensemble** (Lena stays central, and Theo, Laney,
  Margot, Kevin, Becca or Julian keep their beats). Use the extra length for Jack/Skye scenes; don't pad. The Mechanics
  reviewer should treat ~22k as within budget for these episodes.


**[SRC brief.md · Skye: the game's first near-explicit intimacy (no sex; the pressure; confession); Skye's Facebook · L833–858]**

- **Skye: the game's first near-explicit intimacy (player directive).** Skye's arc is the first time the game goes to
  **near-explicit intimacy scenes**: **no sex**, but everything else on the table, raw and hungry, written frankly
  with real sensory and emotional detail at the brief's literary standard (HBO/literary fiction, the specific
  people, never pornographic mechanics). Both are adults (18+).
  - **She keeps trying to get him to have sex**: seduction, plain asking ("unafraid of want"), escalation. **Jack is
    pushed to the brink often.** The resolve system bites hard here (Jack's resolve drops; choices let the player hold
    or slide right up to the line; the line itself never breaks, per HARD RULE).
  - **Consent stays clean, and she keeps trying (player):** she never coerces; in the moment his "no" (or "not that") [see the later refinement: the no covers sex only]
    stops things. But **she never takes the no as final**: another night she tries again, persuasive, playful and
    sometimes less clothed, because **being refused frustrates her and she kind of enjoys it** (the first "no" she's
    ever met that isn't a negotiation becomes a game she can't stop playing). Her persistence is the pressure on Jack's
    resolve; it is desire and play, never force or guilt-tripping.
  - **Confession:** Jack confesses to **Fr. Osei** that he **keeps putting himself in the near occasion of mortal sin**.
    Osei's counsel is real pastoral wisdom (the seal holds; he works only from what Jack confesses), not a scolding; it
    gives the player real choices about whether Jack changes anything. This recurs: the cycle of falling, confessing and
    returning is part of the "true and hard" faith.
  - Lena knows or suspects enough to be furious (through Jack's eyes only), and later this becomes part of Jack's
    honesty with her.
- **Skye's Facebook (player).** Skye isn't a modest dresser and happily shares photos of her and Jack on Facebook.
  **Jack gets tagged in something that sets tongues wagging** (a beach or pool shot, a hotel-balcony morning, Jack
  shirtless with Skye in a bikini wrapped around him, an LA party; suggestive, not explicit). The ripple runs through
  everyone: Laney screenshots it in disgust; Theo "likes" it politely; Margot sends a sociological comment; Kevin winces;
  **Lena** sees it (off Jack's screen; he catches the aftermath); **the Newman crowd** notices (Tyler, Kenzie, Mrs. Fitch
  via a grandchild); **the Costigans** (Gina first, then Maria, then Nonna via rosary); maybe Fr. Osei raises an
  eyebrow at the next confession. Jack untags himself too late. Period note: Facebook tagging and the "untag" option
  exist in 2009–10; the photo spreads via the News Feed.


**[SRC brief.md · Skye Era balance Jan–Feb; politics in their argument life; the alliance's escalating emergency meetings; Skye heat-scene escalation · L951–994]**

- **Skye Era balance, Jan–Feb 2010 (player directive).** Skye's public side (gratuitous Facebook posting, immodest
  style, suggestive posing, antics; comic and scandalous) must be **balanced with the intellectual spark** that drew
  Jack in from the first conversation and **the rare vulnerabilities that deepened it**. **January and February are a
  real, meaningful relationship**, not a fling and not a cautionary tale. They argue books and people for hours, play,
  confide, and she's genuinely good for him in ways (saying it out loud, daring, being seen in the moment) even as the
  faith gap and the physical pressure persist.
  - **Lena's sideways fights target the antics** (the photos, the clothes, the posting, the publicity) **because that's
    easier to criticize**. **What actually bothers her far more is that it's real**: she can see Jack is happy, that
    Skye makes him laugh and talk, that they have their own jokes and arguments. Play the gap: Lena attacks the surface;
    the player and Jack (eventually) understand she's hurt by the depth. Jack defends Skye on the substance, which only
    makes it worse. Payoff in New Orleans (Lena admits it was never the photos).
- **Jack and Skye: politics becomes part of their argument life (player).** After the fundraiser, politics joins books
  and people in their running intellectual sparring. Skye is an LA liberal by upbringing and industry, sharper and more
  self-aware than Jack expects (she knows the hypocrisies of her world firsthand). Jack is the right-leaning Rust Belt
  Catholic of the politics map. Topics fitting Jan–Feb 2010: health care (the bill's fate after Scott Brown wins
  Massachusetts, Jan 19), *Citizens United* (Jan 21), the State of the Union (Jan 27), abortion and the Stupak fight,
  unions and the auto bailout (Frank, Lordstown), immigration, Hollywood and money. They genuinely enjoy it; she
  argues to win and to learn, and her "explain it to me while we walk" rule continues. It contrasts with Jack and
  Lena's arguments: with Skye politics is a game between people who differ; with Lena it's family (same faith,
  different conclusions), which is why Lena's version cuts deeper. Verify dates with the Period Researcher.
- **Running bit: the alliance's escalating emergency meetings (player).** Three, each bigger than the last, seen only
  in fragments from Jack's POV:
  1. **Secret December (Theo suspicious):** Jack catches a glimpse or fragment: Theo and Laney huddled somewhere odd,
     breaking apart when he walks in ("We were discussing… finals.").
  2. **After Jack and Skye are Facebook official (January):** a **bigger** emergency meeting. Jack sees more of it (a
     whiteboard hastily flipped, Kevin dragged in against his will, Margot taking notes "for science").
  3. **After one of Skye's public antics (late January or early February):** the **biggest** emergency meeting,
     which **secretly results in getting Lena to plan the group New Orleans trip** for the Super Bowl and Mardi Gras.
     Jack only sees the outcome: Lena suddenly organizing the trip with suspicious enthusiasm, Laney and Theo
     innocent. The alliance's masterstroke. Confirmed at the champagne reveal.
- **Skye heat-scene escalation (player; refines "first near-explicit intimacy").** New in the series compared with
  Becca: **frequent heat scenes**, as frank and explicit as the brief's literary standard allows **short of sex** (the
  hard rule). They **steadily escalate** across the arc: lengthy kissing (E16–E17) → make-out sessions that get
  **continually more physical, raw and hungry** (the secret December) → **Skye's increasingly effective seduction
  attempts** (January–February), each pushing Jack's resolve closer to the line (resolve drops; the player chooses how
  close he lets it get; the line holds). Each heat scene must also carry character (her play, her presence, the moment
  she drops the act, his guilt and want), so it's never filler. Track escalation across episodes so no two scenes play
  the same beat. Consent stays clean per the earlier directive. Lena sees only the public surface (photos), never these
  scenes.
  - **Caveat (player):** the escalation isn't strictly "seduction only in Jan–Feb." **Her first attempt to sleep with
    him happens in December** (the secret stretch, at her place, the first time she brings him home). He says no, and
    it leads to the lengthy conversation about why (his faith, all of it) and **her first big moment of vulnerability**
    ("scared he thinks she's a whore"), as designed earlier. After that, January–February brings the increasingly
    effective repeat attempts.


**[SRC brief.md · Julian's song -> Jack withdraws to Skye · L1032–1040]**

- **Julian's song → Jack withdraws to Skye (player).** Hearing Julian's song for Lena (E20) wounds Jack, and he
  **completely withdraws into Skye** from then **until the last night with Lena** (E22). He's there in body for the
  Newman party and the rest, but he spends every free hour with Skye; Lena's night-time seeking finds him gone. **Skye
  notices something hurt him but doesn't push** (her presence-without-pressing is part of her pull). This withdrawal
  can **contain, lead up to, or build on** Skye's first attempt to sleep with him, his first "no", the long conversation
  about why, and her big vulnerability moment (room's call on exact placement within E19–E21; the earlier directive
  puts that scene at her place, the first time she brings him home). The finale's empty-campus walk is then the first
  real time with Lena since the song, which is why it's the best night of the semester and why the airport-and-Skye
  ending stings.


**[SRC brief.md · No consent beats unless needed; What Jack's no covers with Skye; Season 2: Jack tells his mom first (the original entry) · L1057–1071]**

- **No consent beats unless needed (player, standing).** Going forward, **don't write explicit consent beats** (asking
  permission, checking in, "is this okay," narrated pauses for consent) **unless the situation would otherwise read
  as sexual assault.** Desire, mutual escalation and Jack's "no" stopping things remain as written; it's the
  procedural check-ins that go. Supersedes any earlier "consent guard" phrasing that called for on-page beats; the
  hard rules (no coercion, no assault, the line never breaks) are unchanged.
- **What Jack's "no" covers with Skye (player, standing; refines "Consent stays clean").** His no is **about sex
  only**, not intimacy. It does **not** have to end the making out or the rest of what they're doing.
  - **The first time is the exception:** the first no stops everything, because Skye needs to understand what just
    happened (the long conversation about why).
  - **After that:** she might stop everything if she gets frustrated enough, but since it's a game to her (she's never
    been told no except as a negotiation), **she most likely doesn't stop. She respects the no strictly to the letter**
    and keeps everything else going, right up against it. That's where the pressure on his resolve comes from.
- **Season 2: Jack tells his mom first (player).** Because his mom scolded him for finding out about Becca second- or
  third-hand from Facebook, **when Jack and Skye go Facebook official he texts or calls his mom first** so she hears it
  from him this time. He does **the same when he and Lena become official** (callback; **she notices he learned**: player-approved detail).


**[SRC brief.md · Season 2: Laney brings the laptop (the mirror of January); Skye's picture texts · L1127–1153]**

- **Season 2: Laney brings the laptop (player; off Jack's screen, relayed later).** The first week back from New Orleans,
  before Jack asks Lena out: when Jack's Facebook goes from "In a Relationship with Skye" back to single, **Laney brings
  her laptop to Lena to show her, watching Lena's face carefully, and asks what she knows about this.** Per the POV
  rule, Jack hears it **from Lena on one of their dates**, likely relayed as a story on herself.
  - **Mirror:** in January Laney *took the laptop away* from Lena over the Skye status; now she *brings it to her*.
  - **Irony:** Lena has given up Facebook for Lent, so she only sees it because Laney shoves it at her.
  - **Lena's poker face fails (player-approved).** She tries to look blank and can't (the same way she can't keep a
    straight face when Jack asks her). Laney reads it instantly ("Oh my God. *Lena.*"). On the date, Lena tells it as
    a story on herself, and Jack gets to picture Laney's face.
- **Skye's picture texts (player; from S1E19 on, especially S2's Christmas break).** Skye sends Jack **provocative
  pictures of herself that escalate over time** (within the hard rules and the brief's explicitness standard; it
  feeds the resolve pressure).
  - **Period tech:** MMS was common by 2009, but the **iPhone only got US picture messaging on Sep 25, 2009** (AT&T), so
    it's a new trick for her phone. **No front-facing camera until the iPhone 4 (June 2010)**, so this is the **mirror-pic
    era**: bathroom/bedroom mirror shots, phone visible in frame, or blind arm's-length shots with the back camera. Her
    whole run with Jack falls before the iPhone 4.
  - **Jack's end:** they arrive small, grainy and slow on the RAZR (about 25¢ each on a basic plan), viewed in the cold
    garage, a stairwell, under a blanket. The RAZR has no real lock, so a house full of Costigans at Christmas is a
    standing hazard (room's call on any near-discovery).
  - **Near-misses: room's call.** But **family and friends (especially Theo and Lena) notice Jack has become far more
    private about his phone from December through February** (tilting it away, screen-down, pocketing it fast, keeping
    it on him at all times). An extension of the secret-December "texting no one" behavior.
  - **New Orleans: the most salacious one yet** arrives **right after he sends Skye to voicemail** (the "phones tell on
    them" beat). Her reply to being ignored.
    - **FLAG FOR THE ROOM (player likes it; room decides):** consider making this a player choice (open it, or delete
      it unopened), with stat and resolve effects. **The room decides whether it fits, how his response plays given his
      arc at that point, and whether Lena is watching.**

**[SRC brief.md · Episode length from S1E19 on; S1E20: Jack's answer to Skye's fear ('a no right now') · L1189–1219]**

- **Episode length from S1E19 on (player; raises the Skye-episode allowance).** Episodes run **longer from S1E19
  onward**: Skye needs a **ton of screentime without cutting into Lena or the ensemble**. Her time is **added on top**,
  not taken from theirs. **Working target: ~22–26k words per path (player-approved)** (no padding; every added scene earns its place).
  - **From the New Orleans trip on, Skye's extra time transfers to Lena**: from that week Lena becomes even more a
    part of Jack's life, so the episodes stay long and the extra share is hers.
- **S1E20: Jack's answer to Skye's fear (player's beats; adapt to voice, not verbatim; EXPAND on all of it).** When
  Skye, suddenly reserved after his first no, voices her fear that he thinks she's a slut (or the like):
  1. **He puts his arms around her first.** She **stiffens**: she expects him to say nothing, because she assumes the
     answer is yes and he just won't say it.
  2. **First: no.** He doesn't think anything like that about her.
  3. **For honesty's sake:** he definitely doesn't like thinking about her with another guy, but that's a completely
     different thing (his jealousy, not a judgment of her).
  4. **The Church's teaching is first for judging his own conduct, not other people's.** He's supposed to tell the
     truth about it plainly when asked (which he has), and to be **hard on himself and generous with others**.
  - **Accuracy/period notes for the room:** the idea is real Catholic teaching (CCC 1861: we can judge an act to be a
    grave offense, but must entrust the judgment of persons to God's justice and mercy; Matthew 7, the speck and the
    beam). **Do not use "Who am I to judge?"**: that's Pope Francis, July 2013 (anachronism). The room may let Jack
    reach for Lewis, Aquinas or his own family (Danny) in his own voice, as long as it stays plain and unpreachy.
  5. **The line he'll regret (player; adapt to the moment and his voice, not verbatim).** At the end, Jack says some
     version of: **"I'm not saying the answer's always going to be no, not until marriage. Just that it's a big thing
     for me, and it's a no right now."** She immediately tells him **she can work with that.**
     - **It sets her approach for the whole relationship:** a no is a no **only for that instance and only about
       sex**. (This is the source of "to the letter.")
     - **Jack regrets it in a small way for years**, and is **more grateful that it didn't make him break**. It puts
       the load more equally on Jack: he opened the door himself.
     - **Skye is never a villain or actually cruel** for what she does after. Both of them sometimes call it "cruel,"
       **always as a joke**: they mean she's incredibly hard, almost painfully hard, to resist, and both know it.
     - **Echo with Lena, years on (player-approved; room places it).** The line returns once: Jack confesses it to
       Lena, or she learns it another way. It's the moment Lena understands **how close he came, and how much he
       held**. Room decides when (after they're together; e.g., the restraint arc's height or the marriage
       conversations) and how it lands.

**[SRC brief.md · Skye's managed public life meets Jack (the four); why she starts posting recklessly · L1264–1284]**

- **Skye's managed public life meets Jack (player; S2, January 2010 on).** Her public life is tightly scripted and
  managed, so Jack becoming her public boyfriend is **a thing for her team too**. All four of these land (room places
  and paces them):
  1. **The team vets him.** Her publicist/manager wants to know who he is before anything goes public: a lunch that's
     really an interview, a Google of his name, rules about photos and what he says. Skye finds it humiliating and
     funny; Jack is out of his depth.
  2. **She goes official herself.** The team wants a slow, managed reveal; **Skye changes her Facebook status herself,
     without asking**: the one unscripted thing in her public life. That's why it's sudden, and why it means more than
     Jack knows.
  3. **Gossip sites notice.** A paparazzi or fan shot (beach, the Saab, maybe Mass) turns up on a gossip blog or a
     "who's her mystery guy" item (period: TMZ, Perez Hilton, Just Jared, blind items; Twitter is new). **Gina finds it
     first.** Lena sees the public surface this way (photos, never the private texts).
  4. **The Danny problem.** Her team finds out his brother is in prison: a tabloid hook waiting to happen for the
     network's "good girl." **Skye refuses to care**, and it becomes a quiet test of who she is. (Never a cheap villain:
     the team is doing its job.)
  - **Why she starts posting recklessly (player).** The team's photo rules are the reason: she's **fed up with them**,
    and she **liked how changing her status on her own felt**, so **she keeps doing it with photos of the two of
    them**. The first ones are **cute and fun**; as they're out being **more wild and reckless**, **the photos get that
    way too**. This drives the Facebook tags, Lena seeing the public surface, and the public antic that sets off the
    alliance's third emergency meeting. (Public posts stay Facebook-plausible; the private pictures stay private.
    Jack's behavior in them stays within his character.)


**[SRC brief.md · E21 design answers (player) — Skye's second attempt; Osei's arc across confessions (tender -> gentle but pointed -> bracing); the Newman party and the stolen glance; the January chain from it; Pictures: a notch up when apart · L1288–1307]**

- **E21 design answers (player).**
  - **Skye's second attempt: the argument.** She out-argues him (takes his own "right now" apart; persuasive, funny,
    less clothed while she does it). **When he says no, she stops only the further escalation** and keeps going **as
    they were before she started whatever he said no to** (the first "to the letter" in practice).
  - **Osei's arc across confessions.** E21: **tender** (CORRECTED below: not loneliness), speaking more to what's good in it than to the sin, because
    Jack stopped it and had a real conversation with her about it. **As Jack keeps putting himself in that position,
    Osei becomes gentle but pointed** ("you're not avoiding the occasion, you're scheduling it"), **then eventually
    bracing.** Never a scolding.
  - **The Newman party: Jack avoids Lena; the distance is the scene.** One **stolen glance later in the evening**, which
    **Lena clocks immediately and correctly as hurt**. That glance is **her opening for their extended night in E22**
    and **the start of her considering breaking up with Julian**, which she decides over Christmas. (POV: Jack gets
    none of this until the New Orleans conversation.)
    - **The January chain from it:** when Lena learns about Skye, **she decides she imagined the glance** (she saw
      what she wanted to see). So when Laney reports basically the same thing in Jack's own words (the January
      aside), Lena is **upset and angry**: she believes Jack is now **lying to play games or to hurt her**, or at best
      Laney misunderstood, because there's no way the man in increasingly intimate pictures with a blonde starlet
      could feel that about her. **January and early February are really bad for Jack and Lena.**
  - **Pictures:** **a notch up when they're apart for consecutive days** (E21). **E22: a big jump** in the picture
    she sends as he's about to head home (her voice, not verbatim: "so you have something to look forward to in
    January…"). That's the finale's buzz.


**[SRC brief.md · S2 Christmas break: the call; Skye and Jack: keep the whole mix (incl. CORRECTION: Jack is not lonely); Becca's reaction to Skye · L1308–1337]**

- **S2 Christmas break: the call (player; adapt, frank at the brief's literary standard, never pornographic
  mechanics).** During a long late-night phone call over the break (Youngstown; the cold garage, his room, wherever
  he can be alone), **Skye tells him slowly and exactly what she's going to do to him when he doesn't say no.** He
  **doesn't stop her**, and **ends up telling her the same** (she likes it; **he's definitely not as good at it as
  she is**).
  - **Why he lets it run:** he **literally can't say yes** to what he's been avoiding, so he lets this go on and on as
    the only yes available.
  - **Too late, he realizes** it's just going to make the real yes easier: in the moment he **feels like he needs it
    now**, weak and powerless, as if this is the only thing he could possibly do.
  - **It passes a little while after they hang up, but he's shaken by it afterward.** (Resolve hit; the line itself
    never breaks. Likely feeds the next confession, where Osei moves from tender toward gentle but pointed.)
- **Skye and Jack: keep the whole mix, every episode she's in (player; standing through the end of their
  relationship).** The relationship is **sex appeal + intellect + wit/playfulness + unrestrained fun**, all four, all
  the way through. **Jack is falling for her hard, and for every part of her**, and **she is falling for him too.**
  Never let the heat/resolve thread crowd out the arguments, the jokes, the dares and the plain joy of being with
  her. Each Skye episode should carry at least one beat of each (Voice Editor and Showrunner check this).
  - **E21 confession content (player).** Jack tells Osei that **she was curious and receptive** to what he said, that
    she was **worried what he thought of her**, and that **she said she'd meet him in daylight, teach him to surf**.
    It all sounds hopeful, and it's part of **why Osei is tender this time** (alongside Jack having stopped and
    talked it through). Osei can gently take up the daylight (his own "keep her in the daylight" counsel, answered).
  - **CORRECTION: Jack is not lonely (player).** He loves Lena and is hurt he can't be with her, but **Skye is
    entirely different from Becca: Jack *wants* to be with Skye.** He enjoys her company, enjoys doing things with
    her, and enjoys **learning about her, body and mind**. Never write Skye as a consolation or a cure for loneliness.
    **Osei's tenderness in E21 speaks to that**: he hears a young man who genuinely delights in this girl and is
    trying to do right by her, and speaks to the real good in it (love wills the good of the other) more than to the
    sin, which is exactly why the occasion is so dangerous.
- **Becca's reaction to Skye (player; S2, January).** Becca knows the breakup was really about Lena (and has never
  told Lena she knows). When Jack and Skye go public, **she's kind, almost unbearably**: she **congratulates him,
  sincerely**, which is worse than anything she could say. **Only later, as friends** (spring), does she admit she
  didn't understand it. She still never brings Lena into it.


**[SRC brief.md · The 'what are we' talk (player; lands in S1E22) · L1347–1351]**

- **The "what are we" talk (player; confirms accelerated-sequence step 8; lands in S1E22, their last night together
  before break).** Jack and Skye's **big in-person talk about what they are to each other** must be a **full scene**,
  not a line: she suggests they **stay like this a few more weeks** because **Christmas, New Year's and long
  distance** are a lot of pressure on a new relationship, and that they **become a thing when he gets back in
  January**. (It's the sincere version of the strange hours: she's protecting something she wants to keep.)


**[SRC brief.md · Skye heat ladder, standing rule (incl. 'the heat stays after the no'); After S1E21; S1E22: the withdrawal has to come up; Jack's double state; The friends notice the withdrawal; S2 Christmas break: Jack is honest with Danny; After S1E22 · L1359–1400]**

- **Skye heat ladder, standing rule (player).** **Every new intimate scene with Skye escalates the heat**, and **each
  no is harder** for Jack than the last (resolve, body, argument, timing).
  - **Exception for explicitness: the Christmas-break phone call is by far the most explicit scene** of the Skye arc,
    even though it's only talk, because **it's the only time actual sex is described** (not happening in the scene).
  - **The in-person heat still keeps rising every episode after it** in the continuing intimate scenes (January,
    early February), each a step past the last, short of sex (HARD RULE).
  - Showrunner and Voice Editor track the ladder per episode (no two scenes the same beat; each step named in the
    outline).
  - **Supersedes `outlines/s1e22.md`'s "E22 does not escalate."** The finale's Skye scenes (the Tuesday to-the-letter
    beat; the Thursday "what are we" goodbye) must also step up past E21, kept proportionate so the night with Lena
    stays the episode's heart.
  - **The heat stays after the no (player).** A no doesn't cool the scene: the heat continues at full intensity,
    **confined to the limit set** (to the letter), rather than ending or fading out. (Except the first no, which
    stopped everything because she needed to understand.)
- **After S1E21 (player feedback).** "Both wonderful and upsetting: everything with Skye was incredible; everything with
  Lena was painful." The player went **full withdrawal**: non-responsive to Lena, and **missed the Saints game at
  Sully's for the first time**.
- **S1E22: the withdrawal has to come up (player).** On the last night, they have **something just short of an honest
  conversation** about it. **She doesn't press, because she saw his face at the party** the one time they looked at
  each other. What she names (Lena's voice): **he's never just not answered her texts; he's never missed a Saints game
  at Sully's; he always used to come to her no matter what was going on, and now he stays away**, speaking to her only
  when he couldn't avoid her. **She knows he's hurting badly**, even though next month, once he's dating Skye, she won't
  believe it anymore. (Jack can't say why; it stops just short. POV: he sees her restraint, not her reasons.)
- **Jack's double state (player; the emotional engine through early February).** The player felt it the whole of
  S1E21, as Jack does: **truly happy, having fun, falling in love when he's with Skye; pure agony when he's with Lena
  and Julian.** Keep **both halves fully true** and side by side. Never let the Skye half curdle into guilt-tinted
  half-joy, or the Lena half soften. The whiplash between them is the experience, until New Orleans resolves it.
  - **The test of it (player):** "When he's with Skye, I can't see how he'd possibly give this up in New Orleans. When
    he's with Lena, I can't see how he wouldn't." **Hold that balance exactly** until New Orleans. The choice there must
    cost him something real, and the Skye ending must be worthy of her.
- **The friends notice the withdrawal (player; S1E22 and into S2).** The group, **especially Theo and Laney**, has
  noticed how Jack has acted **since the song**. Before, it was strange hours, disappearing and reappearing; **this is
  different: complete non-engagement with anything, especially Lena.** Show it in their behavior around him in the
  finale (Theo's quiet worry, Laney's alarm, Kevin and Margot's sideline reads) and let it inform the alliance's
  Christmas-break prep and January ops. POV: Jack sees their looks and changed handling of him, not their talks.
- **S2 Christmas break: Jack is honest with Danny (player).** During a prison visit over the break, Jack is **open about
  what's going on**: not giving details, but honest (Lena, Skye, the song, the withdrawal, the double state). Danny is
  the one person he tells. (Danny has met Lena; room decides his response, in his voice: plain, unsentimental, the man
  who "doesn't wish, he decides.")
- **After S1E22, the Season 1 finale (player feedback).** "Yet again, **Skye was incredible** and I loved every moment
  with her. **Lena was gut-wrenching**; almost moved to tears when she recapped December" (the porch naming of the
  withdrawal). Season 1 complete.

- **S2E02 design answers (player).**
  - **New Year's Eve midnights.** **Both Lena and Skye text him at his midnight (12:00 Eastern) AND at their own

**[SRC brief.md · **End of break: the last misreading (player; S2E02 tail -> S2E03)** · L1441–1453]**

- **End of break: the last misreading (player; S2E02 tail → S2E03).**
  1. **Answering Skye first sits uncomfortably**, so through the end of break Jack **talks to Lena more and more**,
     trying desperately to get a read on where her head is.
  2. **Lena takes his words and actions as an understanding of where they are.** She **stops mentioning Julian**,
     because of how Jack reacted on the phone to Julian compared to everything else she said.
  3. **On one of the last days, she says something about Julian, trying to hint that they're going to be done.** **Jack
     misreads it** as the same reporting she's always done, maybe even a signal to back away a little.
  4. **So their last phone call before Skye goes public ends a little awkwardly.**
  5. **Jack is hurt again**, looks through the pictures Skye has sent him, **decides he's lucky after all, with a
     smile**, and messages her **something a little softer than their usual banter**. (The next thing is January and
     the status; Lena's hint pays off in New Orleans, when he learns what she meant.)
  - POV: Lena's intent stays off-screen; the player sees her words exactly as Jack hears them, written so they read
    both ways on a replay.


**[SRC brief.md · **January–February: Jack and Skye as a real couple (player)** · L1454–1466]**

- **January–February: Jack and Skye as a real couple (player).** Jack **throws himself into being who he could never be
  for Becca**. Not over the top (they both still have lives), but **both are there for each other's things**, and they
  have **time together with physical intimacy, studying, intellectual argument and playfulness**.
  - **Skye comes with him to ensemble scenes**, so **the friend group witnesses it** and **takes note especially of the
    intellectual arguments**, because they're used to Jack and Lena doing that. (Laney and Theo clock it hardest;
    Margot may find Skye's arguments genuinely good; Kevin quietly unhappy.)
  - **Lena is beyond upset and furious about all of it**, and **privately blames Jack for poisoning her relationship with
    Julian**: she was ready to end it for Jack, and now can't get her own head past that with Julian while they're still
    together. (POV: Jack sees only her fury's surface; the blame comes out in New Orleans.)
  - **Their relationship develops quickly on every front (player).** By New Orleans **they clearly love each other.**
  - **They say it (player): Skye says it first**, late January or early February, unguarded, the first time she has
    ever meant it off-screen. **Jack says it back, and means it.** It makes the end after New Orleans cost everything
    (and must be honored in how it ends; never retconned as less than real).


**[SRC brief.md · The thematic key to the Skye/Lena choice (player-approved) · L1467–1482]**

- **The thematic key to the Skye/Lena choice (player-approved).** Jack feels the whole river with both of them (the
  player's reference: HIMYM's made-up *Lebenslangerschicksalsschatz*, the instantaneous lifelong-treasure feeling, vs.
  *Beinaheleidenschaftsgegenstand*, "almost the thing you want"). **Skye is never written as "almost the thing."** Both
  loves are real and both were instant (Lena: the porch and the Mass collision; Skye: he crossed the room himself).
  - **The difference is not intensity but direction.** Skye is face to face: she loves him, delights in him, argues with
    him and respects his faith. **Lena is face to face *and* side by side**, wanting the same life (the sacrament, the
    kids, the whole hard saying). Saint-Exupéry (1939, period-safe): *"Love does not consist in gazing at each other,
    but in looking outward together in the same direction."* In New Orleans Jack chooses the one he can walk toward God
    with, and does not stop loving the other when he does. Not a lesser love traded for a greater: a real love given
    up for the one that is also his vocation. (Never state this as a thesis; let it be lived.)
  - **Later-season callback (approved):** the HIMYM episode aired in 2011, so no one quotes it in 2010. In 2011 or later
    the group watches it; **Jack goes quiet; only Lena knows why.**
  - **The breakup with Skye (player).** At the end, **Jack tries to explain this to her**, probably badly, because **he
    feels he owes her that**: that **he really is in love with her**, and that **he loved all of what they were and did
    together**. Not a speech, not the Saint-Exupéry line; his own fumbling words, honest, and it doesn't make it hurt
    less. (Her response: room's call within her co-design; never cruel, never diminished.)


**[SRC brief.md · The New Orleans conversation includes Skye, honestly (player) · L1483–1490]**

- **The New Orleans conversation includes Skye, honestly (player).** Since they're fully honest in New Orleans, **Jack
  tells Lena this too** (what Skye is to him, what they were and did together, that it's real), **without using the
  word "love."** Neither of them says "love" on the trip: both know they love each other, but they won't sound like
  crazy people or say it while they're with someone else. **The trick: Jack can't say outright that he loves Skye,**
  because if what he and Lena have is more than that, he'd be saying out loud that he loves Lena. The word stays
  unsaid; the meaning is unmistakable.
  - **It hurts Lena**, and it's **why her dislike of Skye never goes away** (the running joke later has a real root).
  - **At the same time she knows it means he's giving all of that up for her**, because of how he feels about her.


**[SRC brief.md · Skye after the end (player; long arc) · L1491–1499]**

- **Skye after the end (player; long arc).** **Jack and Skye eventually become friends**, but **not close** (that
  would bother Lena, and Skye might not be able to handle it either). **It takes a long time: Skye is deeply, deeply
  hurt by Jack.** But she is **eventually grateful that he told her the truth, and that he did it before anything
  happened** (before they slept together). She stays in the wider world via Theo; the slow thaw is the room's to pace
  across later seasons.
  - **Clarification (player): how Skye takes the breakup is still the room's choice** (acceptance, or fighting back for
    him hard, whichever fits her character). **One option is now OFF the table: that she knew all along and expected
    it.** **She didn't. It was always real to her.** (Her clocking Lena in S1 is jealousy and intuition, never
    foreknowledge or resignation.)

- **Comic bit after the Skye breakup (player): Laney reads the tabloid version aloud.** She both **makes a show of it**
  and **genuinely relishes it**: a gossip blog / weekly item on the breakup (period: Perez Hilton, Just Jared, Us
  Weekly, a "sources say" blind item), gloriously wrong about everything ("a source close to the actress says the
  mystery college boyfriend 'couldn't handle her fame'"). Dramatic reading in the lounge; Theo supplies sound
  effects. Keep it on Laney and the tabloid, never on Skye's pain (Laney grudgingly liked her; one flicker of that
  under the glee). Room places it (S2, after New Orleans).


**[SRC brief.md · Lena's apology for the song (player; New Orleans conversation ONLY) · L1506–1509]**

  everything open), **Lena apologizes for asking Jack to come listen to Julian's song** (S1E20 s13: "Come tonight…
  don't leave in the second chorus"). **She knows that's when everything changed between them.** She didn't do it to

**[SRC brief.md · S2E02 mid-episode (player) — Frank's deadpan comedy is landing · L1510–1512]**

- **S2E02 mid-episode (player).** Laughed out loud at **Frank asking about Skye's swimsuit picture while the family
  watches her show** ("usually it's Laney who gets that out of me"). Frank's dry, deadpan family comedy is landing:
  give him more of it in Youngstown scenes (never at the cost of his sobriety arc or his weight).


**[SRC brief.md · Why Jack goes to New Orleans at all (player; all four stack) · L1513–1525]**

- **Why Jack goes to New Orleans at all (player; all four stack).** By early February he has just told Skye he loves
  her and is as happy as he's ever been, while he and Lena have spent a month alternating between silence and
  fighting. He would not want to go. Four things get him on the road, layered across the late-January episodes:
  1. **The Saints.** A Rust Belt football kid and a once-in-a-lifetime Super Bowl run in New Orleans (the game is in
     Miami; the city is the place to watch it). Even furious, he can't pass it up, and **tells himself that's the only
     reason.**
  2. **Theo asks, for himself.** Theo, who never asks anyone for anything, asks Jack to come as a personal favor ("I
     need you there"), maybe already paid. Jack can't say no to that. The alliance's real play, played straight.
  3. **The Broussards invite him.** Not Lena: her family. Mémère (whom he has spoken to) or her mother calls or writes
     to him herself. You don't say no to Mémère. (Room decides whether Laney nudged it; Lena didn't.)
  4. **Skye tells him to go.** She's away that week anyway (location shoot or press; room picks a plausible reason) and
     pushes him: go watch your Saints win. **She trusts him completely, which is the knife: it was always real to
     her.**


**[SRC brief.md · **Jack tells his mom first, and she brings up Lena (player; S2E03/E04)** · L1526–1533]**

- **Jack tells his mom first, and she brings up Lena (player; adapt to voices, not verbatim; S2E03/E04, right before
  Jack and Skye go official).** On the call (or text, then call) where Jack tells Maria about Skye first, as he
  promised himself, Maria points out that **he was texting and calling Lena all break too** (calling her whatever
  Maria calls her on the page: check continuity, e.g. by name, "the Saints girl" is Frank's, or a Maria-ism).
  Jack: **"She's still got a boyfriend, Ma. He wrote a song for her. It was good."**
  - **Maria doesn't say anything, because she can hear it in his voice still.**
  - **Jack can tell she wants to say something but can't figure out how**, and **he can't figure out what.** The call
    ends on that silence. (Joins Danny's and Nonna's unanswered questions: now three people who see it.)

  together and the show opens up: their relationship stays central but develops *around* everything else going on
  (mystery, friends' arcs, family, the world). Showrunner: name the background beats advanced in each S2 outline.


**[SRC brief.md · The Reliquary goes back to Lena in New Orleans (player) · L1539–1547]**

  and **won't get a chance to give it back before Skye goes public**, and once Skye is public **Lena absolutely would
  not want it** (if he tries, she refuses it or he can't bring himself to offer). **She gets it back in New Orleans.**
