  admitted at last at the wedding); **Lena's running joke has a real face** ("Maybe Skye would agree with you, Costigan." — #80). Never a villain; she was
  right about how afraid Jack is, and wrong about why.


### Theo


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L494–499]**

**Private truth. 🔒** Theo was at Marisol on the night of April 25–26, 2009 (Preview Weekend). He is the one who was on the
cliff when Nora Calloway fell. (§20.) He has not slept more than four hours at a time since. His hedonism is
self-punishment wearing a party hat. He chose Marisol over USC *because* of the Point — he could not be anywhere else. He
leaves fresh flowers at the Point cross every Sunday before dawn. In July 2009, during freshman orientation, he walked into
St. Brendan's Newman Center for the first time in his life and made his first confession since he was eight. Fr. Osei
has known since July what his new parishioner's roommate did.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L501–502]**

He latches onto Jack the moment he sees the crucifix go up on the wall. Jack is the nearest thing to grace he can stand
to be near without being looked at by it.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L504–504]**

**Wound.** The girl's jacket in his hands. Under that: a father who taught him that every problem has a fixer.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L506–506]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to be punished without being known. *Need:* to be known and survive it.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L526–537]**

**The alliance (brief; re-planned after S1E11).** From S1 Theo runs a covert pro-Jack-and-Lena alliance with Laney. On
**Jack's** girls he is perfectly polite in the open and privately Team Lena, passing Laney information (Becca's schedule,
E07–E16; Skye's, from S2E03). **The December secret (accelerated after S1E13):** Theo learns that Jack and Skye know each other from
his own News Feed at the Costigans' kitchen table (S1E18; he didn't know she was in Marisol), watches the likes pile up (S1E19), asks
the warzone question at 6:10 a.m. (S1E21) and gets his own September lie back ("Couldn't sleep. Went to look at the ocean.") — and
holds it, even from Laney, until he runs into Skye in LA over Christmas and is sure (S2E02) — then he and Laney come back from the break
well-prepared (brief): the one secret he kept for Skye, for a month. On **Lena's** boyfriends after Wes he is openly, inventively rude — and after Wes there is only
**Julian** (S1E15–S2E11): "the vampire who asks permission," "Magdalena's screensaver"; Jack mostly doesn't stop him, then does
(S2E04): it isn't going to work out, and it's turning mean for no reason. He **knows Skye from LA** (childhood friends: the same Meridian sets and wrap parties; practically siblings)
and knows exactly what she is. Never acknowledged aloud until the reveal at **Floatopia (S2E17)**
(§24.7). **Thanksgiving 2009:** Lena can't go to Youngstown (she's with Julian), so Jack invites Theo, and Theo goes
(S1E16–E18).


**[SRC bible.md · §8.1 Theo — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L539–549]**

**Arc (revised after S1E08 for the brief's "Theo's darkness: mostly hidden").** The darkness stays under the charm —
glimpsed, never staged as a collapse (the 5:20 a.m. returns, the forgiveness question, "Does he catch anybody?", the Sunday
drives, the Point) — until the late reveal. S1: the best friend with a hole in him; the jean jacket in his closet; Evan
circling. S2: rushes Sigma Rho and is very good at it; the anniversary (Evan at the cross before dawn — private, not public);
in the S2 finale tells Jack the truth on the cliff. S3: holds it together in public while Jack holds his secret; a romance
with Laney that is mostly fun (two charmers) with an undertow neither names; no overdose, no scene in which he falls apart in
front of anyone. S4: turns himself in on the second anniversary (April 26, 2011) — the first time Marisol sees anything at
all, and it is a choice, not a collapse. S5: the legal and family fallout; leaves Marisol; meets Nora's parents in Fresno. S6–S7:
sober, working on a documentary about the closing of an American factory — it's about Lordstown, and Frank Costigan is
in it. S8: returns for graduation; receives Confirmation at the Easter Vigil 2013 with Jack as his sponsor (if the path
supports it; otherwise, he stands in the back). SU3: best man (July 21, 2012 — or Danny, if Danny is home; both stand up). 🔒 Theo lives.


### Laney


**[SRC bible.md · §8.2 Laney — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L565–569]**

**Private truth. 🔒** At fifteen she was the passenger in a car driven by her best friend Kaylee Whitmore, who had been
drinking; the car hit a culvert on Preston Road; Kaylee died; Laney walked away with a broken wrist. The Xanax began a
month later. The partying is a way of proving she's alive and making sure she doesn't have to feel it. Lena is the first
person since Kaylee whom Laney has let stay over in her heart. Their friendship is a love story: Laney would die for Lena,
which is the problem.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.2 Laney — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L571–571]**

**Wound.** Kaylee. Being the one who lived.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.2 Laney — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L573–573]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to be adored and never seen. *Need:* to be seen and stay.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.2 Laney — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L588–594]**

**The alliance (brief; re-planned after S1E11).** Theo's co-conspirator. On **Jack's** girls she is openly rude to every
one of them — Becca "on principle" (E07–E16); **Skye** in public (S2E03–S2E11; the December is secret from her), while privately and begrudgingly liking her
(cigarettes on the Pi Lam roof; never admitted). Lena tells her to stop and be nice to all of them *except Skye*, whom Lena
can't stand either. On **Lena's** boyfriend after Wes — **Julian** — she is perfectly polite in the open and privately Team
Jack, passing Theo information. Kevin tells them both to leave it alone; both refuse. The reveal: Floatopia, S2E17 ("The
knee was *art*, sugar.").
**(E09 plan:)** Since the 4:41 text ("LIAR. xo") Jack and Laney have a text rapport, which is why he makes the mistake of


**[SRC bible.md · §8.2 Laney — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L642–648]**

**Arc (revised after S1E08 for the brief: "Laney: mostly fun. The pills and rush darkness stays an undertow; she is the
comic engine. No overdose scare.").** S1: rush, bid day, the Pi Lam world; the Xanax an undertow (Lena counts the pills, E07);
the fight with Lena at midterms (E10); Kaylee told to Jack. S2: New Orleans with the Broussards — Celeste adopts her on sight; her nineteenth birthday is Valentine's Day, Bacchus Sunday (S2E09);
the actuarial secret starts to leak (comic). S3: Theo (mostly fun); **she puts the pills down** — one night on the floor of
their Serra room, Lena with the pillbox in her lap and no speeches, a doctor's taper after (no scare, no hospital). S2E17: the
alliance reveal with champagne in her own hand (moved up by the re-plan); S4: the rupture, from across the hall. S5: actuarial science, out loud. S6–S8: quietly, impossibly, toward the Church — received into full communion at the Easter Vigil
2013 with Lena as sponsor. SU3: maid of honor (July 21, 2012). Adulthood: an actuary who sponsors other women in AA.


### Margot


**[SRC bible.md · §8.3 Margot — private truth / wound / want / arc · L667–670]**

**Private truth. 🔒** She's lonely in a way her parents' freedom taught her to call independence. She has watched her
mother humiliated in her own kitchen by her father's "honesty." She envies Jack and Lena's certainty and hates that she
does. Anders. She is bisexual and has never said so to anyone, because in her family it would be met with a *reading
list*. She is the best investigator in the show.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.3 Margot — private truth / wound / want / arc · L672–672]**

**Wound.** Anders. Her parents' freedom.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.3 Margot — private truth / wound / want / arc · L674–674]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to win. *Need:* to be accompanied without being argued with.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.3 Margot — Arc (head line + S2 tail from L691) · L690–727]**

**Arc.** S1: the antagonist who keeps showing up; the clove wake (E07); she and Lena's 2 a.m. abortion argument that ends
[… earlier (S1) history omitted by the Packer: bible.md L691–L690 …]
with both of them crying and neither conceding. S2: starts the Nora anniversary feature for the *Tide*. S3: dates Evan
Marsh (not her TA) and gets closer to the truth than anyone. S4: publishes — responsibly, and it forces the reckoning; she
is right, and it costs Jack and Lena everything for a while. (She likes Julian and tells Jack he's the healthiest thing in Lena's life — S1E16, and means it.) At the wedding (SU3) she signs the register as "witness for the prosecution." S5: the seal story; she and Jack fight about the confessional
like it's a war. She never converts. Adulthood: an investigative reporter (ProPublica-type); godmother to one of Jack
and Lena's children, at her insistence, "as a hedge."
**Anders, as she tells it (E07 plan — the Delgado roof, Wed Sep 23, ~3 a.m., to Lena, Jack and Kevin; 🔒 until then).**
Spring 2000; Margot was nine. Her mother was forty-one; an amniocentesis at about sixteen weeks; trisomy 21. Her parents
decided at the kitchen table and told Margot the same night "in age-appropriate language" (her father's phrase, which she
quotes like a diagnosis). They had already picked the name: Anders. The procedure was on **March 15, 2000** — "the ides; my
father pointed that out; he thought it was interesting." She has defended her mother's choice in every forum since, and
means it; she keeps the date alone every year, and nobody has said his name aloud since 2000 but her. **Never a strawman
in either direction:** she is not converted, softened or "won"; she grieves a brother and defends her mother in the same
breath, and the show holds both. **The same night, after Kevin walks her down, Kevin comes out to her** — the first person he
has ever told (🔒 off-page; Jack sees them leave the roof together and reads it as a romance).
**(E11 plan:)** On the trip "as an observer" (the monks: "a functioning patriarchy in its natural habitat"; the faith column's monastery profile). In the van
she runs the *Tide*'s Ferrer follow-up as an interrogation (the police log — "one of them was laughing"; from 305 she heard the bass come in late twice;
"Costigan suggested Baptists. I've noted it.") and **writes nothing down.** At Vespers (fork) her arms come unfolded by the second psalm. At the Henry
Miller Library (fork) — the investigator, unprompted — she tells Theo he leaves every Sunday before dawn (she saw the SL go at 2:40 in Malibu, E03, and
she doesn't sleep); she lets his pitch go. At the motel she reads Lena's copy of Dorothy Day's *The Long Loneliness* by flashlight (one line aloud: love
comes with community), sees Jack go out to the porch and Lena follow, and Sunday at the coffee urn says only **"I'm not writing anything down."** In the
turnouts she becomes a Saints fan against her will. Says the balloon was a hoax exactly once: "I said it was too calm."
**(E12 plan:)** **Joan of Arc** — cardboard and silver duct-tape armor over her black, a wooden sword, and her own white-blonde jaw-length bob, which
turns out to be Joan's haircut exactly ("burned as a heretic in 1431, canonized in 1920; I'm both categories; so was the Church"); horns at the door
("the verdict of 1431"). Supplies Laney's Pope Joan footnote and loses the sources argument to Jack and Lena. At the costume reveal: **"Between the two
of them there's one functioning farm."** Best Couple with Laney — "This is a category error." (her suppressed laugh). The investigator, unprompted:
**"Your host left his own party at twelve-thirty. Out the back gate. Toward Seacliff."** (fork A: "Kevin hasn't taken that mask off in four hours." 🔒).
Writes nothing down when the DJ plays the Saints.
**(E13 plan:)** Monday's whiteboard: **HALLOWEEN — AMERICAN GOTHIC (UNPLANNED) · BOTH**. Sully's "for the anthropology." At the Harbor Inn she
interviews Caroline about the 1980s nighttime soap "as a text" and enjoys herself; after Jack's answer to Caroline: "That was very gallant,
Costigan. Gallantry is a system for deciding things on other people's behalf." (She is right about something.) She sees Kevin watch Maine
(🔒 one look; says nothing).
**(E14 plan:)** 🔒 Wednesday, Maine decided: she sets a coffee at Kevin's elbow and says nothing. **The fire-alarm sting fails** (#28): Sat night in
the 4th-floor lounge with Theo's 5D on a tripod and Gold Bond on the pull handle; asleep at 2:50 for the first time in recorded history; Alarm 4 at
3:11 from the east stair (26 days); she stares at the timestamp of her own sleep and never looks at the bottom of the frame (🔒 the heel, the pillow —
Doug, E20). Saturday night by phone to Lena at the Anchor counter, furious about Stupak, **with her best argument** (it isn't federal money; it reaches
into private plans on the exchange that women pay for themselves) — never a strawman; Lena gives her the point and not the baby. At Sully's, of the
courtesy war: **"It's a potlatch. They're destroying property to establish rank."**


### Kevin


**[SRC bible.md · §8.4 Kevin — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L743–748]**

**Private truth. 🔒** Kevin is gay and out to exactly one person, Margot (week four of S1 — before that, no one). He came to
Marisol's Preview Weekend in April 2009, met a blond LA kid on the beach stairs behind the Lighthouse, and kissed a boy
for the first time; they had about an hour. That boy was Theo. Around 12:45 a.m. Theo knocked on the door of Kevin's
host's room in Serra Hall, soaked to the knees, his hands scraped raw, holding a woman's denim jacket, and said, "You
never saw me." Kevin let him sleep on the floor. In the morning a girl was dead. Kevin has never asked. He has never told.
He pretends, when they "meet" on Jack's first night, that he's never seen Theo before. Theo pretends too.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.4 Kevin — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L750–750]**

**Wound.** The family's sacrifice is a debt he can't repay and can't refuse. Being loved for a person he isn't.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.4 Kevin — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L752–753]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to be good — perfectly, visibly good — so that nothing else can be seen. *Need:* to be loved as
he is by someone who isn't lying either.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.4 Kevin — private truth / wound / want / alliance / arc · L768–769]**

**The alliance.** Kevin is the one who tries to get Theo and Laney to leave Jack and Lena alone ("Respectfully, this is
a hostage situation"); both refuse; he keeps trying, every season, and is in on the joke at the reveal.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.4 Kevin — Arc (head line + S2 tail from L775) · L771–801]**

**Arc (revised for the player's directive: a long struggle, shaped heavily by his friendship with Jack and Lena, with real
[… earlier (S1) history omitted by the Packer: bible.md L772–L774 …]
both)**; a stairwell fight with Theo Jack half-hears (E16). S2: Newman VP; pressure from Peter Haddad; Margot as confessor;
**the sad, sincere attempt with a girl** (a Newman sophomore, Clare Dominguez, S2E04–E13 — she deserved better and knew it
within a month; nobody is a villain). S3: comes out to Jack and Lena (they love him, and say the hard true thing, and stay;
Margot is loudly offended on his behalf — at the teaching, and at them). S4: when the Nora case reopens, his night with Theo
becomes evidence — he must decide whether to be a witness and out himself to his family; he does. S5: his father stops
speaking to him; Bà Nội does not. He tries celibacy and the sacraments in earnest, and it doesn't hold yet. S6–S7: a
relationship with a man (Andrés, a med student — kind, serious, not a villain) and a year away from Mass (a semester at the
Episcopal parish in Las Olas). S8: he comes back to sit in the back pew on Holy Thursday 2013. Adulthood: a second
relationship ends; he settles — by his own decision, at full cost — as a celibate gay Catholic; an ER doctor (Osei's
vocation, inherited); godfather to one of Jack and Lena's children. He tells them they are the reason he could imagine it.
**Margot** never accepts the teaching and says so at every table for twenty years, and comes to accept and respect that Jack
and Lena genuinely love him ("I hate that it's real. It's real."). The show accompanies him; it never pretends the cost is
small, and never pretends the life is a defeat.
**(E11 plan:)** Drives his mother's Odyssey ("SPEED: 55 ON HWY 1. NOT 56." on the laminated card; "Switzerland has a *seating policy*"); hands Lena a
roll of quarters for the payphone without being asked; white to the lips when the van boils over ("My mother is going to know. She'll feel it in San
Jose."); "Second gear. The whole way." 🔒 One line only: when Laney's room chart puts him and Theo in the one bed in cabin 5, "I'll take the floor" — at
the same instant Theo says "I'll take the van." Never touched again.
**(E12 plan:)** "**All Saints** — every one nobody's heard of. It's tomorrow; I'm early.": scrubs from the summer program, a halo from Theo's box, and
**a plain white mask he never takes off** until Delgado's door at 2:45 a.m. (🔒 one image; Margot notices on fork A). Alliance: "Respectfully, this is a
hostage situation" (once, at Theo's legal pad). The designated adult at a party of four thousand: **he finds Laney at Lupe's** and calls Lena. On the
path where Jack carries Theo's lie ("Looking for Laney"), Kevin looks at Jack through the eyeholes for a long second and says nothing (🔒 he knows
what Theo looks like when he comes back from somewhere at night).
**(E13 plan:)** quarter-zips again; the mask is gone. The objector at the false dawn: **"Respectfully — her boyfriend broke up with her eleven
hours ago."** (the only voice in 412 on the side of Jack's wait, though nobody knows it's a side). At the Harbor Inn, Maine's Question 1 on the
bar TV — too close to call — and **Kevin's face** (🔒 one image; Margot sees it).
**(E14 plan:)** "Respectfully — leave it alone." (once, at the ficus); 🔒 Maine decided — the quarter-zip zipped to the chin in the sun; Margot's coffee;
at Alarm 4 the clipboard; **lector at the 9 p.m. Mass, reading the November names — *Nora Calloway* twice, because two hands wrote it, steadily** (🔒).


### Fr. Osei


**[SRC bible.md · §8.5 Osei — private truth / wound / want / arc · L813–822]**

**Private truth. 🔒** He was married. **Abena Osei** (née Mensah), a schoolteacher, died on November 14, 1997, on the
Accra–Tema motorway, when Gabriel, driving her to the hospital in labor with their second child, swerved to avoid an
overturned tro-tro. The baby girl (they had named her Esi) died with her. His left leg was crushed; hence the limp. He
was the doctor in the car and could do nothing. Their son **Kwame**, then eight, was sent to Abena's sister in London
(Peckham) while Gabriel recovered — and then Gabriel went to seminary instead of bringing him home. Kwame, now 20, at the
London School of Economics, has not spoken to his father in four years. Gabriel writes him every month.

He holds two sealed confessions about the same night at the Point (🔒 §20): Lena's (April 26, 2009) and Theo's (July
2009). He knows more about Nora Calloway's death than anyone alive except Theo, and he will never say a word of it. Not
to Jack, not to police, not to a judge. The show honors the seal absolutely; it costs him, publicly, in S5.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.5 Osei — private truth / wound / want / arc · L824–824]**

**Wound.** Abena, Esi, Kwame. The choice of the altar over his son.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.5 Osei — private truth / wound / want / arc · L826–826]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to save them all. *Need:* to let God do it, and to be forgiven by his son.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.5 Osei — private truth / wound / want / arc · L874–880]**

**Arc.** S1: the anchor, quietly watching his two penitents circle the boy he cannot warn. S2: the anniversary Mass at the
Point; Hale's public debate. S3: Kwame comes to Marisol (a conference) and refuses to see him; Jack brokers a dinner. S4:
Theo goes to police; Evan, then the press, realize Osei likely knew. S5: the seal on trial (Ireland's Cloyne Report and
Enda Kenny's speech, July 2011, make the seal front-page news); subpoenaed; refuses to testify about the content of a
confession; held in contempt; five nights in county jail; the Newman community splits. S6: the bishop wants to move him;
the students fight; he stays. Full run: he tells Jack the story of the limp in S6. SU3 (Sat July 21, 2012): he witnesses Jack and
Lena's vows at Holy Rosary (by the pastor's delegation). 🔒 Kwame comes to the wedding.


### Hale


**[SRC bible.md · §8.6 Hale — private truth / wound / want / arc · L894–903]**

**Private truth. 🔒** Son of an Anglican vicar in a mining town. Served at the altar until seventeen. Lost his faith the
winter his mother died of ovarian cancer, slowly, while his father remained serene; he has never forgiven the serenity.
He still says Compline, drunk, alone, from his father's prayer book, and would rather die than be caught. On his worst nights
he drinks alone in the Anchor's back booth after midnight, orders pie he doesn't eat, recites Housman to the harbor window,
tips "like he's apologizing," and lets Gloria call him a cab (first on-page: Nora's birthday night, S1E04). He collects
believers: for years he has written one-line postcards to devout high-school students who send him rebuttals of his book —
"Come and say it to my face. —A.H." He wrote one to Jack (2008). He wrote one to Lena (2008). He wrote one to Nora
Calloway (2007). On the night of April 25, 2009, at 11:52 p.m., Nora called him from the Point path. He was drunk. The call
lasted forty-one seconds. He said: *"Go home, Nora. You're drunk and you're boring me."* He told the police she'd called
upset about a paper and he'd told her to sleep on it. That's not quite a lie. It's worse.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.6 Hale — private truth / wound / want / arc · L905–905]**

**Wound.** His mother; his father's peace. Nora's forty-one seconds.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.6 Hale — private truth / wound / want / arc · L907–908]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to win one believer from God, to prove that he was right to leave. *Need:* to be found by the
thing he left.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.6 Hale — private truth / wound / want / arc · L969–974]**

**Arc.** S1: the seduction of two minds; the postcard discovery (E14); the dinner party where he's magnificent and then
vile. S2: debates Lena publicly at the Marisol Forum and loses; loves her more for it. S3: writes a book on moral luck
using his students as unnamed examples; Jack finds himself in a footnote. S4: the forty-one seconds come out; disgrace;
leave of absence. S5: sobriety, attempted. S6: cirrhosis. S8: his last lecture is the Philosophy commencement address.
Adulthood: 🔒 dies in 2015; asks for Fr. Osei; what happens in the room is left ambiguous. The show never claims a
deathbed conversion. It lets Jack see Hale's hand move toward his forehead, and stop, or not.


### Julian


**[SRC bible.md · §8.7 Julian — private truth / wound / want / fatal gap / arc · L1008–1011]**

**Private truth. 🔒** He was Nora Calloway's closest friend and her duet partner; they sang at the Point wall at night
freshman year. He loved her; she loved someone she wouldn't name. He wrote a song after she died — "Fresno" — that he plays
once a month and can never finish without his voice going. He doesn't know about the pregnancy or Evan. He suspects
suicide and blames himself for not seeing it.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.7 Julian — private truth / wound / want / fatal gap / arc · L1013–1013]**

**Wound.** Nora. Being the friend.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.7 Julian — private truth / wound / want / fatal gap / arc · L1015–1015]**

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to be chosen first by someone. *Need:* to stop accepting second place as proof of goodness.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.7 Julian — private truth / wound / want / fatal gap / arc · L1026–1031]**

**The fatal gap (brief: both).** He respects her faith without ever believing it — he stands lovingly outside the church
door, and no sacramental marriage is possible — *and* he's too easy: gentle and deferential, he never fights her, and she
discovers she needs to be argued with. Perfect on paper and wrong in the bones; the ending is sad. **Hard rule:** they
never sleep together; he never asks her to (it's one of the things that makes him heartbreaking). Theo is openly rude to
him until Jack, who can tell it won't work out, tells Theo to stop (S2E04); Laney is polite and feeds Theo; Margot thinks
he's the healthiest thing in Lena's life and says so.


**[SRC bible.md · §8.7 Julian — Arc (head line + S2 tail from L1045) · L1033–1053]**

**Arc (re-planned after S1E11 for the player's exact sequence; co-design folded in after S1E13).** S1: glimpsed on the party stairs; "Fresno" (E04; Lena
[… earlier (S1) history omitted by the Packer: bible.md L1034–L1044 …]
(Mémère calls him *le petit violon*; the Facebook album Jack sees in a Youngstown basement, S2E02 — the song was December's, S1E20). He
reads *Confessions*, then O'Connor, then Percy, for her — thoughtful, never a counterargument. **S1E20 (re-mapped): the song, privately and then public at Bastille (Thu Dec 10); Lena
watches Jack hear it.** Theo rude; Laney polite; Margot: "the healthiest thing in her life" (S1E16); Jack tells Theo to stop (S2E04). Red herring
\#4 lives in Jack's jealousy (S1E15–S2E05): Julian at the wall at dusk; "Fresno." **He doesn't come to New Orleans** for the
Super Bowl or Mardi Gras when she asks: "It's your city. I'd be a tourist in it. Go." — the gentlest possible refusal and the
whole fatal gap in a line. **The end (S2E11, Sun Feb 21, 2010, the First Sunday of Lent):** she ends it, honestly, within a
week of coming home ("I'd lie for you if you'd let me. You won't let me." — and hers, the true one: "You've never once told me
I was wrong."). Theo does not celebrate in front of him. **Hard rule:** kissing at most. **After New Orleans (brief): graceful, and stays a friend** — he takes it like the good man he is and remains in the wider circle; S4 learns what
Lena hid about Nora — the hardest scene he has; **plays at the wedding (SU3)**. Never converts; never stops being decent.


### Wes (the house, not the church)


**[SRC bible.md · §10.1 Wes — who he is / the unraveling / the end / after · L1099–1114]**

**Who he is.** During Katrina, at sixteen, Wes took his uncle's aluminum flatboat out on the third day and ran it through
Gentilly and the edge of the Ninth Ward for four days, pulling people off roofs and out of attics. He is a neighborhood
legend. He carried Lena's twin siblings out of the second floor of a neighbor's house. He has been in love with Lena
since she was fifteen, and he waited until she was seventeen to say so. They've dated since her senior-year Mardi Gras
(February 2009). They are "holding the line" together — his idea as much as hers — and they hold it: they never sleep together (hard rule). He's funny (a gentle, goofy, Saints-
obsessed humor), kind, devout in the way of a Jesuit boy: he can do the Ignatian Examen and the Who Dat chant in one
breath. He's the kind of boyfriend parents pray for.

**The unraveling (sad, not expected).** He is not cheating. He is not a cad. **He is losing God.** On the fourth day of the
boat, behind a house on Pauger Street, he heard knocking under a roofline — a woman in an attic — and couldn't get the
axe through the rafters before the water rose and his uncle made him go to the next house; when they came back in the
morning the knocking had stopped. He has never told anyone: not his uncle, not Lena, not a priest. Four years later,
studying the Army Corps of Engineers' failures in a Tulane classroom, he has begun to believe that the water was just
water and the knocking was just a woman and there was no one listening to either of them. He stops going to Mass in
September. He doesn't tell Lena. He drinks a little more each week. He keeps calling her every night at 10 Central / 8
Pacific, sweet and funny, and she keeps not noticing, because she doesn't want to.


### Becca


**[SRC bible.md · §10.4 Becca — hard rule / arc · L1485–1488]**

**Hard rule:** they never sleep together. Kissing, warmth, her head on his shoulder at the 9 p.m. Mass — and nothing deeper
there. That is the whole tragedy. **Never a placeholder** (brief): Jack already knows how he feels about Lena, so Becca must
have real gravitational weight of her own — she is the whole good life everyone wants for him (his mother, sight unseen:
"Seven! Catholic?"), and on some nights he wants it too; the player must be able to love her.


**[SRC bible.md · §10.4 Becca — hard rule / arc · L1523–1537]**

**(Accelerated after S1E13 — player directive: Jack and Becca end BEFORE Thanksgiving.)** **E14** she says she flies to Spokane the
Wednesday before Thanksgiving ("Mom's already named the turkey"); Maria, on Sunday's call: "Then Christmas! Bring her at Christmas, I'll
make the cardoons." **E15** a week of being a girlfriend at full tilt (the Facebook status; Bastille, clapping on one and three).
**E16** — after Lena tells Jack about Julian — Becca, shining at Coffee & Donuts: her mother says she can fly to Ohio for three days after
Christmas if Mrs. Costigan asks ("I've never seen snow that wasn't *ours*!"); and Jack understands that bringing her into that house would
be a lie bordering on cruel **even with Lena no longer available** — the fence has nothing left to fence. **E16 (Wed Nov 18): Jack ends it**,
kindly and honestly, in the Ranger outside Portolá with the heater ticking: he can't take her home; it would be a lie, and she'd
believe it. She sees — without cruelty, because she is never cruel — that he has been loving her a little in order not to love
someone else, and names it: *"I did everything right. I know I did. It just isn't the thing, is it."* He has no argument. Nobody is
wrong. (Her reaction, room's call: she cries in the cab with the engine running, then makes him take the second jar of huckleberry jam
for Theo, "because it isn't *his* fault.") **E17** at SBA, a week later, at the next gate for Spokane: a St. Raphael card for Nonna — "I'm
still allowed to pray for her, right?" **After:** a friend, carefully; Coffee & Donuts still knows his order; the Sunshine Mine rosary is told as a sweet fact when
they talk as friends (**S2E04**, the Haiti table — re-placed by the S2E04 plan; never before); she marries in the summer of 2011, at twenty — by twenty-one, as
she said — a Veritas missionary (room's call: Tyler Brandt is available), which Margot considers a hate crime; **she comes to Jack
and Lena's wedding (July 21, 2012)**, pregnant with the first of nine, and cries at the vows.


### Evan (Hale's reader)


**[SRC bible.md · §10.7 Evan — secret / what he does · L1667–1677]**

**The secret.** He was Nora Calloway's boyfriend from October 2008 to her death. She was in his section; they told no one
(it would have cost him his funding and her her reputation). She was eight weeks pregnant. On April 25 they fought by
text; at 12:05 a.m. she texted him from the Point: *"I'm at the Point. Please come. I'm keeping it. I want to keep it. I
want you to want it."* He read it at 12:20 and drove. At 12:31 his headlights caught a tall blond kid running from the
Point path toward Seacliff Road clutching something dark. He went to the wall, saw nothing, called her phone, heard
nothing, assumed she'd left to punish him, and drove home furious. At dawn surfers found her. He told police he'd been
asleep and seen her text in the morning; he deleted it. He has spent four months hunting for a tall blond boy.

**What he does.** He is not a villain; he is a coward that night who is now trying to be brave in the wrong way. He sees
Theo at the Halloween chaos (S1E12) and goes cold. He begins asking careful questions (S1E19: "Your roommate — was he at
Preview Weekend last April?"). In S2 he becomes dangerous — to Theo, and to himself.


### Calloways, Carly, Mikey Kostelnik (Struthers)


**[SRC bible.md · §12 — Calloways / Carly Benedetto / Mikey Kostelnik · L2003–2011]**

- **Tom and Rosa (Gutiérrez) Calloway**, Nora's parents, raisin farmers near Selma (Fresno County). 🔒 Their parish in
  Selma whispered suicide. Rosa has not been to Communion since — she believes she failed her daughter. Appear S4–S5.
- **Carly Benedetto**, 19, Youngstown, Jack's friend from Mooney, at Youngstown State. 🔒 The girl Jack hit Mikey
  Kostelnik for (§16, revised: Mikey was her ex, drunk, hitting her and dragging her toward his truck; no sexual
  element). She knows the truth. Appears in Youngstown episodes.
- **Michael "Mikey" Kostelnik**, 20, Struthers, Ohio. 🔒 The young man whose skull hit the cinderblocks. Traumatic brain
  injury: seizures, slowed speech, can't drive or work, lives with his mother Donna. Jack sends anonymous postal money
  orders to Donna Kostelnik every month from his Anchor wages.
  **(S2E02 plan:)** 🔒 Donna goes to the 9:30 Mass at **St. Nicholas, Struthers** [V — else a fictional parish] and sits in the back; Carly sometimes sits behind her. On New Year's Eve 2009 Carly asks Jack, "Do you ever hear how Mikey's mom is doing?" — he goes white; she tells him about the back pew (never "Donna," "Kostelnik" or money on the page). Seeds S7 (the truth told to Donna first).


## 4.3 The bible's hidden architecture


**[SRC bible.md · §13 Hidden prior knowledge at the pilot (Knowledge Auditor: read this) — who already knows what before S1E01 · L2023–2051]**

\## 13. Hidden prior knowledge at the pilot (Knowledge Auditor: read this)

At 6:48 a.m. on August 29, 2009, before the first line of the pilot, these people already know things the player doesn't:

- **Lena** knows: Nora Calloway (her Preview Weekend host, April 24–26, 2009); the Lighthouse house and the path to the
  Point (Nora walked her past it on Friday night, April 24); Nora was pregnant (the test in the trash); that she herself
  removed and discarded the test; that she told police she slept through; that around 12:45 a.m. she heard a boy crying
  in the Serra Hall corridor and a door shut, and didn't look. Fr. Osei (she confessed to him April 26). Mrs. Fitch (met
  at the Preview brunch). Hale (postcard, 2008). She does **not** know Theo, Kevin, Evan, Julian (she knows *of* Julian
  — Nora mentioned "my friend Jules who writes songs"), or Jack.
- **Theo** knows: what he did; Kevin (the beach stairs, the floor of Kevin's host's room); Fr. Osei (his confession, July
  2009); the Lighthouse; the Point; Nora's name (from the news). He has never seen Lena (she was asleep in Serra; he never
  saw her). He has Nora's jacket in a duffel in his closet.
- **Kevin** knows: Theo, and what Theo looked like at 12:45 a.m. He does not know what happened. He knows Nora's name
  from the news and the Newman memorial Mass. He has seen Lena once (the Preview Weekend Newman brunch, across the room)
  and might recognize her. He knows Fr. Osei (Preview brunch; a summer email).
- **Fr. Osei** knows: Lena (her confession); Theo (his confession); Kevin (brunch); Nora (his parishioner and soprano).
  He knows, from the two confessions, more than anyone. He can use none of it, and he will not even let it change how he
  speaks to them in a way that would betray the seal.
- **Hale** knows: Jack (his nine-page letter, 2008); Lena (her twelve-page letter, 2008 — longer, he notes); Nora (his
  student; the forty-one seconds). He has never seen Jack or Lena's faces. He knows their names are on his PHIL 9H roster.
- **Evan** knows: Nora; the pregnancy; the text; the blond boy (not his name or face clearly).
- **Priya** knows: Nora (freshman roommate); the box.
- **Rafi** knows: he sold Nora two bars that night.
- **Julian** knows: Nora; the wall at the Point where they sang.
- **Mrs. Fitch** knows: Nora; Lena (Preview brunch: "the New Orleans girl who asked for the mantilla basket").
- **Sloane** knows: Nora; that Nora was "seeing someone older"; that Nora left the party around 10:50 p.m. upset.
- **Margot, Laney, Jack**: know none of these people and nothing about Nora.


**[SRC bible.md · §14 Secrets register (ensemble) · L2345–2375]**

**[SRC bible.md · §15 The elision rule (how close third keeps Jack's secret honestly) · L2376–2386]**

\## 15. The elision rule (how close third keeps Jack's secret honestly)

Jack is the POV and the player *is* Jack, so the narration must never lie about what Jack did. It may **elide**: it can
decline to narrate a memory. Jack thinks about Danny constantly; the narration says so. It shows Jack flinching from the
scar story, smoking when Danny's name comes up, sending money orders to a "D. Kostelnik" (S1E05, a detail with no
explanation), writing letters he doesn't send. It never says "Danny did it" in Jack's voice; it never says "Jack did it"
until the reveal. When the truth comes out (S2E22), every earlier scene must read as honest in retrospect: the player
should feel *Jack was carrying this the whole time and I was carrying it with him without knowing.*
- Allowed: "Jack doesn't think about that night. He's good at it."
- Not allowed: "Jack has never hurt anyone."


**[SRC bible.md · §17 JACK'S SECRET (full) · L2444–2474]**

\## 17. JACK'S SECRET (full)

**What happened:** §16. Mikey Kostelnik lived. He has a traumatic brain injury. Struthers PD found Danny with blood on his
shirt and Jack's scholarship-track future on the bench in the back of the cruiser. Danny had a prior (a 2006 bar fight,
misdemeanor assault) and was on probation; the Mahoning County prosecutor charged felonious assault; Danny took a plea
for five years in January 2008. At the station at 4 a.m., Jack, sixteen, tried once to tell a detective the truth; Frank
walked in and took him out of the room by the back of his neck. On the bench in the hall, Jack prayed for the first time
in his life like someone was there. He has been praying like that ever since. **His faith was born the night he became a
coward.** That's the knot.

**Why it matters to the show:** Jack spends seasons judging Theo's silence — and Lena's — while living inside his own. The
central mystery is a mirror: everyone who loves Theo will be asked what Jack's family already chose. Hale's specialty is
*moral luck* (Williams, Nagel): the idea that we are judged for outcomes we didn't control. Jack threw one punch in
defense of a girl; Mikey hit a cinderblock. Theo grabbed a girl to save her; she fell. Hale's seminar will put the
question to Jack in the abstract in S1E05 and he will argue it like his life depends on it, and the player won't know
why yet.

**The money orders.** Jack sends $20–$60 monthly to Donna Kostelnik, unsigned. Shown without explanation from S1E05.

**The clock.** 🔒 Jack is aware, vaguely and with dread, that there are time limits on prosecutions, and that if he does
nothing long enough, nothing can be done. *Writers: never specify the law on the page. The dread is moral, not legal.*
The show's position: he will tell, and the telling (S7, Fall 2012, to Mikey's mother first, then to the Mahoning County
prosecutor) costs him; what the law does about it is less important than what Donna Kostelnik does — 🔒 she slams the
door, and then, in S8, she comes to Danny's welcome-home Mass.

**Danny's release:** judicial release granted November 2011 (S5) — or the full term to January 2013, depending on whether
Jack has told the truth by then (S5 path flag). Danny at Jack's graduation, May 2013. Danny as godfather to Jack's first
child. **(Re-planned after S1E11:)** Lena knows the truth before the proposal (S5 at the latest; the player may tell her from S3).
Danny stands up at the wedding (July 21, 2012) only if he is out; if not, Jack and Lena drive to Trumbull's visiting room the
week they're home, in the suit and the dress (room's call) — the path's price, paid in a cinderblock room.


**[SRC bible.md · §18 LENA'S SECRETS (full) · L2475–2519]**

\## 18. LENA'S SECRETS (full)

**A. The Preview Weekend (tied to the central mystery).**
- April 24, 2009 (Friday): Lena, 17, arrives for Marisol Preview Weekend. St. Brendan's runs a hosting program for Catholic
  admits; her host is **Nora Calloway** (Serra Hall 3B; Sloane was away for the night at the Pi Lam formal in Santa
  Barbara). Nora is luminous, funny, kind, a soprano; they stay up talking about Catherine of Siena and boys. Nora walks
  her past the Lighthouse to the Point at sunset on Friday and shows her the wall: "This is where I come to yell at God."
  Lena falls a little in love with the idea of becoming her.
  🔒 **(Added after S1E13, for E14's postcards:)** over Nora's desk in Serra 3B, pinned with a thumbtack, is **Hale's 2007 postcard** — the
  Radcliffe Camera, *Come and say it to my face. —A.H.* Lena, who has the same card in her missal at home, laughs out loud; Nora laughs too: "He only
  writes the ones who mean it. I'm taking his seminar. I said it to his face *twice*." (Nora takes the card down Saturday night; it goes into her
  jacket pocket — §20.5.) **So when Hale says at his table that he has "used three," Lena knows exactly who the third was** — and that is the medal
  (thinking) Jack sees and can't read (E14). She never says so; the narration never connects it.
- April 25 (Saturday): Nora is distracted all day; disappears for an hour after lunch (she bought the test at Walgreens,
  2:14 p.m.). Newman admit dinner 6–8 p.m. At 10:30 p.m. Nora goes to "a party for an hour"; asks Lena to come; Lena
  declines (she's seventeen, tired, and a good girl in a strange town). Nora returns at 11:15, shaking, goes into the
  bathroom; Lena, pretending to sleep, hears her crying and praying the Memorare. At 11:40 Nora kneels by Lena's air
  mattress and whispers: *"Cover for me, Magdalena. If anyone asks, I'm in the shower."* Leaves.
- 11:45 p.m.: Lena gets up for water and sees the test in the bathroom trash. Two lines.
- ~12:45 a.m.: Lena hears a boy crying in the corridor — a broken, gasping sound — then a door down the hall open and shut.
  She doesn't look. She's covering.
- April 26, 7:40 a.m.: the Serra RA knocks: there's been an accident. Before police arrive, Lena takes the test out of the
  trash, wraps it in a Newman bulletin, walks it to the dumpster behind Serra Hall, and comes back. To Det. Ochoa she says
  she was asleep by eleven and Nora was "fine. A little sad. Normal sad." She touches her grandmother's medal the whole
  time.
- April 26, 2:00 p.m.: she goes to St. Brendan's and makes her confession to Fr. Osei: she lied to the police and she
  threw away something that belonged to a dead girl. He absolves her and tells her, gently and firmly, that she should
  tell the police the truth. She doesn't. She tells herself she's protecting a dead girl's name and a family in Fresno. 🔒
  The irony she doesn't know: the autopsy already told the police about the pregnancy. Her sin hid nothing. Her guilt is
  real anyway.
- She turns down Notre Dame (partial aid) for Marisol (Regents' Scholarship, full tuition) and tells everyone it was the
  money. It was also Nora: "I owe her four years."
- She never told anyone about the boy crying in the corridor. She has thought about him every night.

**B. Grandmère and Memorial (family rupture).** See §10.9. Lena knows her grandmother died at Memorial during Katrina; she
knows her mother was working in the building and left on a boat on the fourth day; she has never asked her mother a single
question about it. She has read every public article about the Memorial deaths and hates herself for it. Celeste's text
on August 30, 2009 (*"Don't read the Times this week, cher."*) is the first time her mother has acknowledged that there is
anything to read.

**C. Wes.** She knows something's wrong. She has noticed he hasn't mentioned Mass since Labor Day. She's decided not to
notice. **(Re-planned after S1E11:)** On All Souls (S1E13) she asks, and he tells her, and ends it (§10.1).

**D. The postcard.** Hale wrote her the same line he wrote Jack. She came, in part, to say it to his face.


**[SRC bible.md · §20 THE CENTRAL MYSTERY — "THE POINT" (20.1 surface · 20.2 who she was · 20.3 the truth of the night · 20.4 layers of guilt) · L2538–2617]**

\## 20. THE CENTRAL MYSTERY — "THE POINT" 🔒

The player chose "surprise me completely." This is the spine that will entangle the ensemble from the pilot through at
least S5, with consequences through the full run.

\### 20.1 The surface (what Marisol believes)
At about 6:10 a.m. on **Sunday, April 26, 2009** (the Third Sunday of Easter), two surfers paddling out at the Point break
found the body of **Nora Grace Calloway**, 19, a sophomore, on the rocks below the old Point Marisol Light. The coroner
found a BAC of 0.09 and a therapeutic level of alprazolam. Her phone was in her jeans, destroyed. Marisol PD (Det. Sgt.
Ray Ochoa) ruled it an accidental fall: she was known to sit on the seaward side of the low wall at night. The campus held
a vigil. St. Brendan's held a memorial Mass. A white wooden cross stands at the wall, and every Sunday before dawn
someone leaves fresh flowers there; nobody knows who. Privately, many — including her parents' parish in Selma, the
detective, Julian, and later Margot — believe it was suicide.

\### 20.2 Who she was
Nora Grace Calloway, born **September 8, 1989** (the Nativity of Mary) in Selma, California. Father Tom, a raisin farmer;
mother Rosa (Gutiérrez), a school-district bookkeeper; two younger brothers (Mateo, 15; Sam, 12). Philosophy major, Music
minor, Newman choir soprano. Funny, generous, intense, devout, doubting, brave. Freshman year in Delgado 222 with Priya;
sophomore year in Serra 3B with Sloane. She wore a Levi's jean jacket with a **Fresno Fair pin** on the collar and an
embroidered **Our Lady of Guadalupe patch** sewn on the back by her grandmother. She sang with Julian at the Point wall.
She sent Hale a rebuttal in high school and got his postcard. She took his seminar. She fell in love with his TA.

\### 20.3 The truth of the night (timeline)
- **Oct 2008–Apr 2009:** Nora and Evan Marsh, secretly together.
- **Sat Apr 25, 2:14 p.m.:** Walgreens on Mission Street: pregnancy test. Positive (about 8 weeks).
- **6:00–8:00 p.m.:** Newman admit dinner (Nora hosts Lena).
- **~8:30 p.m.:** Nora texts Evan the news. He calls; they fight — he panics, says "we can't," says "there are options."
- **10:30 p.m.:** Nora goes to the Lighthouse party (the last house on Seacliff Road, rented by senior surfers). Julian is
  there with his guitar; they sing one song on the stairs ("Skinny Love"). Rafi Ochoa, a high-school senior then, is
  selling. Nora buys two Xanax bars from him: she hasn't slept in a week. She takes one with a beer. Sloane sees her leave
  at ~10:50, crying.
- **Also at the Lighthouse:** Theo Vance, 18, an admit, crashing with an LA friend's older brother who lives there. Around
  10:45 he goes down the bluff stairs to the beach to smoke and meets **Kevin Tran**, 18, an admit, who wandered out of
  the party because he couldn't breathe. They talk for an hour. They kiss. Kevin leaves ~11:50 for his host's room in
  Serra Hall, shaking with joy and terror. Theo stays, does two lines of cocaine alone on the stairs, and walks out along
  the bluff toward the light.
- **11:15 p.m.:** Nora back at Serra; the bathroom; the Memorare. **11:40:** "Cover for me, Magdalena." **11:45:** Lena sees
  the test.
- **11:52 p.m.:** On the Point path, Nora calls Hale. Forty-one seconds. *"Go home, Nora. You're drunk and you're boring
  me."*
- **12:05 a.m.:** Nora texts Evan: *"I'm at the Point. Please come. I'm keeping it. I want to keep it. I want you to want
  it."* She sits on the seaward side of the wall, legs over the drop, as she has done a hundred times, and prays aloud.
- **~12:25 a.m.:** Theo, high, comes up the path in the dark and sees a girl on the outside of the wall, talking to
  someone who isn't there. He thinks she's about to jump. He runs at her and grabs her from behind by the jacket. She
  startles, twists, her hand slips off the wall; the jacket pulls off her shoulders as she goes; he's left holding it. She
  doesn't scream. He hears her hit. The last words he heard her say were *"…never was it known that anyone who fled to thy
  protection…"*
- **~12:27–12:31:** Theo, in shock, runs. Down the Point path toward Seacliff Road, the jacket clutched to his chest.
- **12:20:** Evan reads the text and drives. **12:31:** at the Point lot his headlights catch a tall blond boy running
  toward Seacliff Road holding something dark. Evan goes to the wall; it's black; he calls her phone (it's under her, in
  the surf, already dead); he waits ten minutes, decides she's left to punish him, drives home. He deletes the text the
  next morning. He keeps a screenshot on his laptop he took at 12:21 to "show her later how scared he'd been." It's still
  there.
- **~12:45 a.m.:** Theo walks to Serra Hall — the only place on campus he knows anyone — and knocks on Kevin's host's door
  (Serra 3F, two doors from Nora's room), soaked to the knees from crossing the creek mouth, palms scraped raw from the
  path, holding the jacket. He's crying. *"You never saw me."* Kevin lets him in; he sleeps on the floor; he's gone at
  dawn. **This is the crying boy Lena heard.**
- **6:10 a.m.:** surfers find her.

\### 20.4 The layers of guilt (no villains)
Every person who touched that night believes, privately, that they killed Nora Calloway:
- **Theo** — he did, by trying to save her. The truest guilt and the least culpable intent.
- **Evan** — he said "there are options," didn't answer at 12:05, saw the boy, drove away, lied.
- **Hale** — forty-one seconds.
- **Lena** — she covered for her; she didn't stop her; she hid the test; she didn't look at the crying boy.
- **Rafi** — the Xanax.
- **Kevin** — he let Theo in and never asked.
- **Julian** — he didn't see she was drowning.
- **Sloane** — she let her walk out of the party crying.
- **Priya** — they drifted.
- **Ray Ochoa** — he closed it as an accident while believing it was suicide, out of mercy, and that mercy kept the truth
  buried.

The show's answer, delivered over seasons and never as a speech: everyone did a small wrong thing, the fall was an
accident, and Nora **was not trying to die** — she had chosen to keep her child and was praying the Memorare on the wall
where she always prayed. The proof is two things only a guilty man and a coward kept: Evan's screenshot of the text, and
Theo's memory of the prayer. The truth heals one person completely — Rosa Calloway, her mother, who has believed for two
years that her daughter killed herself and her grandchild. In S5, Rosa receives Communion for the first time since April
2009. That's the payoff the whole mystery is built for.


**[SRC bible.md · §20.7 Seed plan — the E22 photograph and the S2 (Spring 2010) paragraph · L2864–2879]**

- **E22 (S1E22 plan rev. 1 — supersedes the bullet below where they differ):** the photograph is **a print in a letter from Rosa Calloway** (she writes every December to the girl Nora hosted; this year it came to the Newman office and Fr. Osei gave it to Lena after the Gaudete Mass — 🔒 the priest who holds Theo's confession under the seal handing on a letter about the jacket; nothing on the page). **Fri–Sat night, the Point (s15), inside the walk:** Lena goes past the last house for the first time all semester; Nora on the wall, looking back over her shoulder, laughing — Our Lady on her back, the red Ferris wheel on the collar; "It was my weekend. The Saturday." (new to Jack); **"I don't know what she was wearing. I was asleep."** — her hand to the medal (🔒 the police lie; recorded, never read; S2E05's lie-tell note makes it legible). **Jack has held that jacket — one sentence: his hands, the shelf, the knot in the thread, somebody taking it out of his hands; no Theo, no Prague, no Evan, no Halloween, no conclusion; never on a card.** No smash to black — the season ends on Skye's buzz (brief); Jack carries the silence to the roof and the airport and tells no one. Theo leaves Friday for LA; over Christmas he moves the duffel (as below).
- **E22 (season cliffhanger):** On the last night before break, Lena shows Jack a photo on her laptop: Nora at the Point
  wall, laughing, in her jean jacket — the Guadalupe patch, the Fresno Fair pin. "They never found it. Her mom asked me in
  a letter if I remembered what she was wearing." Jack has seen that jacket. Smash to black.

**S2 (Spring 2010):** Over Christmas Theo moves the duffel (his mother's pool house in La Cañada). **S2E04 (Tue Jan 12; re-mapped from "S2E03"):** Jack checks the
closet — gone (and, Tuesday night, finds the Facebook group *In Memory of Nora Calloway* — Julian, Priya and Lena are members; Theo is not; its wall is grief, never clues — no post about the flowers, the cross or the jacket's loss; [`joined_nora_group`] the News Feed carries it to Theo 🔒). He investigates in silence, alone, while his life fills with Skye, the Saints and New Orleans; Margot starts the
anniversary feature (S2E12) and, later, he with her. On the trip, one beat only: Luc, NOPD, at the Broussards' kitchen table,
on how cops close a case they feel sorry for (🔒 Ray Ochoa's mercy, unnamed). **S2E16:** the duffel is back on the shelf after
Easter; Jack opens it and finds Hale's postcard to Nora in the jacket's inside pocket → the Hale suspicion (red herring #1),
sharpened because Lena has just beaten Hale at the Forum (S2E13). **S2E17:** Priya opens the journal (the April 23 entry). **S2E18,
the anniversary (Sun Apr 25 – Mon Apr 26, 2010, Good Shepherd Sunday):** Evan confronts Theo at the cross before dawn with the
flowers in his hand; Jack, following Theo for the first time, intervenes; Evan knows now. **S2E22:** Theo tells Jack the truth on
the wall. Jack, in exchange — because it's the only thing he can offer — tells Theo about Danny. (The player learns both truths in
the same scene.) **Jack is Lena's boyfriend by then, and now he carries a secret that isn't his.**

## 5.2 Season map (bible §23) — the intro/crosswalk, §23.1 (S2E03), and §23.2 (S2E04 and S2E05)


**[SRC bible.md · §23 intro, season numbering, crosswalk, supersessions · L4025–4048]**

\## 23. WINTER BREAK, SEASON 2, SUMMERS AND LATER SEASONS

*(Re-planned after S1E11 for the player's MAJOR RE-PLAN, exact sequence and long-range timeline: together in Spring 2010;
sophomore year a couple with restraint emerging; junior year restraint at its height, the proposal and wedding planning;
married the summer of 2012; senior year married and living together; the 2012 election, Benedict's resignation and
Francis's election in their last semester; graduation 2013. The central mystery and every hidden arc keep their spine; only their
timing against the romance moves.)*
***(RE-MAPPED by the S2E01 Showrunner pass, 2026-10-23 — brief "Season 2 runs about 30 episodes" and "Season numbering": Season 2 is now
~30 episodes, mapped episode by episode below (§23.1–§23.2); summers are numbered seasons and every heading and episode id in §23.3–§23.12 has
been renumbered. The superseded 22-episode Season 2 map is kept for the record in §23.13.)***

> **Season numbering (player directive; APPLIED in this section).** **S1** Fall 2009 · **S2** Christmas 2009 + Spring 2010 (~30 episodes) · **S3** Summer 2010 · **S4** Fall 2010 · **S5** Spring 2011 · **S6** Summer 2011 ·
> **S7** Fall 2011 · **S8** Spring 2012 · **S9** Summer 2012 (the wedding) · **S10** Fall 2012 · **S11** Spring 2013 · then adulthood (AD). "Season 3+" in older directives means Summer 2010 onward.
> **Crosswalk for the REST of the bible** (§6–§8, §10, §17, §20.7, §21, §24 still carry the old labels — read them through this table until a dedicated pass renumbers them):
> - **Old season labels → new:** "SU1" → **S3** · "Season 3"/"S3" (Fall 2010) → **S4** · "Season 4"/"S4" (Spring 2011) → **S5** · "SU2" → **S6** · "Season 5"/"S5" (Fall 2011) → **S7** · "Season 6"/"S6" (Spring 2012) → **S8** ·
>   "SU3" → **S9** · "Season 7"/"S7" (Fall 2012) → **S10** · "Season 8"/"S8" (Spring 2013) → **S11**. (E.g., old "S3E18" = **S4E18**; old "S4E17" = **S5E17**; old "S5E22" = **S7E22**; old "S6E09" = **S8E09**; §17's "S7, Fall 2012" = **S10**.)
> - **Old S2 episode numbers (the 22-episode map) → new:** old E01–E02 → **E01–E03** · old E03 → **E04–E05** · old E04 → **E06–E07** · old E05 → **E08–E09** · old E06 → **E10–E11** · old E07 → **E12–E13** · old E08 → **E14** ·
>   old E09 → **E15** · old E10 → **E16–E17** · old E11 → **E18–E19** · old E12 → **E20–E21** · old E13 → **E21** (the Forum) · old E14 → **E22** · old E15 → **E23–E24** · old E16 → **E25** · old E17 → **E26** · old E18 → **E27** ·
>   old E19 → **E28** · old E20 → **E29** · old E21–E22 → **E30**. (E.g., registry #58's "S2E17 Floatopia" = **S2E26**; #89's "S2E03" Haiti table = **S2E04**; §10.2's "near-miss (S2E06)" = **S2E11**; "the end (S2E11)" = **S2E18**.)
> - **Supersessions the re-map carries (brief wins):** (1) **the first kiss is at the end of date #1, and Lena kisses him** (brief, "the asking," refinement 5) — **not** at the Easter Vigil; the Vigil is where it becomes **official**
>   ("a yes without an asterisk"); Lena's "given up kissing for Lent, in principle" is **retired**. (2) **They DRIVE to New Orleans** (~30 hours; the six of them in Kevin's Odyssey; brief "what flips the trip") — not Theo's
>   plane tickets; Kevin and Margot do **not** fly in later. (3) **Proxy fight #2 is the drive** (the worst, no exit) — not the Broussards' Valentine's kitchen. (4) **The trip is the alliance's masterstroke** (brief, emergency meeting #3):
>   Lena "suddenly" organizes it after a Skye antic — not a spontaneous invitation after the NFC game. (5) **Date #2 is not on a Lenten Friday** (brief: their dates avoid Fridays in Lent) — St. Joseph's altar on Fri Mar 19 is a feast
>   with everyone, and date #2 is Sat Mar 20. (6) **The asking is after the Friday Stations at St. Brendan's** (brief refinement 3 — St. Brendan's steps), not the east stairs.


**[SRC bible.md · §23.1 The Christmas break — the Youngstown Christmas (S2E01–E03) · L4050–4056]**

\### 23.1 The Christmas break — the Youngstown Christmas (S2E01–E03; brief: follow Jack home; "Christmas break: 3"; registry #64, #84, #90)
Lena is in New Orleans (the default; with Julian, so no visit — availability windows); Jack is in Youngstown, secretly going to be dating Skye in January (ACCELERATED SEQUENCE step 8, E22). Long calls, a mailed gift, a Facebook
message at midnight Mass; **the Christmas-break calls** (brief) are warm and charged on Jack's side and neither says what they mean — 🔒 to Lena they're part of why she decides, over Christmas, to leave Julian (off-screen; New Orleans tells
it). Skye on the RAZR and the family Dell; **the pictures climb a notch while they're apart** (escalation by *his* situation). Theo starts the break in LA and asks to come for Christmas Eve and Day. **Theo runs into Skye at the party in the
hills on Dec 22** (E22's plant) and Skye calls Jack about it. **The Costigans find her.** **The Christmas-break call** — the most explicit scene of the arc, words only — breaks two priests' counsel in one night. He flies back Jan 9; she
picks him up with a sign; her team vets him; she changes her status herself; he calls his mother first. **(S2E03 rev. 2: the break episode now runs through Mon Jan 11, the first day of
classes — Becca's congratulations, Lena's formality at Hale's table, Skye's first night with the friends, and the first in-person rung of January.)**


**[SRC bible.md · §23.1 S2E03 · "Epiphany" — the season-map entry for THIS episode · L4062–4062]**

**S2E03 · "Epiphany"** · **Fri Jan 1 – Mon Jan 11, 2010** · Mary, Mother of God (holy day); **Epiphany (Sun Jan 3: the Magi "departed for their country by another way")**; the Baptism of the Lord (Sun Jan 10); the first Monday of Ordinary Time (Jan 11 — classes begin). *(Full plan: `outlines/s2e03.md` **rev. 2** — pre-production; S2E01 PLAYED (flat); S2E02 published and unplayed, every gate exhaustive on the published names; supersedes rev. 1 and the paragraph that stood here.)* **The spine is the brief's end-of-break misreading:** answering Skye first at his midnight sits wrong, so **Jack calls Lena more and more** (New Year's Day from the garage at the Rose Bowl's half; Sunday to tell her how the third went, as he promised; every night after); 🔒 **she takes it as an understanding of where they are and stops saying Julian's name** (he listens for it through two hours on Saturday with Julian asleep across the hall in Remy's room; it never comes; the album shows him at the table); **Fri Jan 8, the last call (the garage): she hints that she and Julian will be done — "I haven't been fair to him… I'm going to fix it."** — written to read both ways; **Jack misreads it** as the reporting she's always done, and a door shut gently on his nightly calls; the call ends a little awkwardly (he's the one who ends it); hurt, he goes through Skye's pictures, decides he's lucky after all, with a smile, and sends her something softer than their banter (`s2e3_soft`). 🔒 New Orleans tells him what she meant. **The week:** Fri — Nonna's lentils (Frank's bowl out for two); OSU 26–17 — "Pryor." · Sat — **St. Brigid's confession line, the fork** (`s2e3_conf`; Fr. Dom: "Just." — a voice in the dark spent at the wrong ear); **THE TWO-HANDER BY PHONE: Lena calls the Glenwood landline ("I wanted to hear your house"), Maria answers** ("She wants you. I don't know why."); the long cord into the pantry; **two hours on her mother's dying cordless**; Grandmère's christening gown; Wes to the house, not the church (🔒 §10.1); **"Be the priest" — the baptismal promises rehearsed** (`s2e3_promises`; [Unguarded] he says *I do* with her; nobody names it); *The Moviegoer*'s ending; "My brother asked me something at Christmas I can't answer yet."; **"How's California?"** (her only time); **"Come back, Youngstown."** · Sun (Epiphany) — **Trumbull, Maria's Sunday: Frank goes in again** (by `s2e2_danny`); "They'll win." — Browns 23–17, "Four straight."; **Lucille baptized in the third quarter** (texts; no Julian line); Jack's evening call pays "Tell me how it goes on the third" (by `s2e2_room`) · Mon — **Tess's album on the basement Dell: Julian at the Broussards' table, *Mémère and le petit violon*** (the name she hasn't said, in sixty-one pictures); Gina: "Who's the guy?"; Hale's PHIL 109H syllabus (cc Lena; opposite ends of the table) · Tue — **Skye's team call** (Corinne and, for the first time, Debbie, rehearsed at him; Herod a jealous showrunner, the Magi the first fans; "who picked February") · Wed — the Befana's sugar coal; the lights down; the Stroh's fridge full of Canada Dry (this save: "Your mother likes the bubbles."); Twelfth Night — Lena got the baby · Thu — **Danny's letter: IS SHE BLIND OR DID YOU LIE**; **rung 8** (§10.2) while he answers it · Fri — **the last call; the misreading; the pictures; the softer text** · Sat Jan 9 — the door (Nonna's "*Prego per tutt'e due*"; Maria's empty Café du Monde can for "the girl who called"; Gina: "Don't let Ma find out from Facebook."; Frank at PIT: "Call your mother when you land."); **SBA 7:52 p.m.: OFFICIALLY in eyeliner**; the drive ("Tomorrow matters"; [unopened rungs] opened with her watching his face); **the Anchor — Laney's "ninth"** (🔒 op one; the index card; the orange hat; boysenberry; one probe) · Sun Jan 10 — **the team in a West Hollywood back room** (Debbie, Corinne, the Google, the rules, February's managed sighting; "religious *religious*?"); **the COSTIGAN folder pushed back unopened** (item 4 seeded); "Then you're going to be very expensive."; Lena's landing text in a men's room ("Come find me tomorrow, Youngstown."); **Buellton: she sets her status herself; it waits on his confirmation; he walks to the air pump and calls his mother first — "Is she still asking?" (`s2e3_ma`); "You told Danny before me…"; then Maria brings up Lena — he was on that phone with her all break — and Jack: "She's still got a boyfriend, Ma. He wrote a song for her. It was good." — she says nothing; the call ends on that silence (brief, newest; Maria the third who sees it) — then Confirm**; 🔒 Lena sees it ≈8:15 and Laney takes the laptop (off-screen); **Laney leaves 412 with her laptop and says nothing**; Theo: "Monday is going to be a long day." · **Mon Jan 11 (new to the episode): St. Brendan's 12:10 — Osei at the door by `s2e3_conf`; BECCA'S CONGRATULATIONS on the steps** (sincere, almost unbearable; she never brings Lena into it — `s2e3_becca`); **Hale's first PHIL 109H (Mon/Thu 1:00, Hollis 302) — Lena at the far end, perfectly pleasant, for anybody; the king cake FOR THE SEMINAR; "It's on the table, Jack. Same as everybody's."** (her formality begins on the page; Laney at 217's door that morning: "She's out."); **Frank's deadpan** through the Youngstown scenes (brief); Mémère on the Friday call — "When you come to New Orleans you come to this house first." (seed of the four reasons he goes); background beats named in the outline §2; **the Fishbowl at eight — Skye's first night with the friends** (Theo, Kevin, Margot, Doug, Priya at the sign-in; *The Bachelor* and the McGwire news [V]; **the argument — whether a sentence somebody else wrote can be true when you say it**; Margot sides with her; Kevin goes to the machines; Doug: "Dude. This is like you and Lena."; **Laney is not there — "She's in 217 tonight."**; Laney's first sighting stays S2E04's); **Del Playa — heat rung S2-a** (§10.2; `s2e3_rung`); the tag: he tells Skye two of his mother's three questions. **The two questions (Danny's, Nonna's) stay unanswered**; both asymmetries play (Lena: "How's California?" / Skye: the third question kept). **Rung 8; S2-a.** The king cake piece never reaches Jack. End card: *“They departed for their country by another way.”*


**[SRC bible.md · §23.2 Season 2 — Spring 2010: title theme, allocation, standing threads · L4064–4073]**

\### 23.2 Season 2 — Spring 2010 (classes Mon Jan 11 – finals May 10–14) — S2E04–S2E30
*Title theme: "Do you also wish to go away?"* Liturgical Year C: Ordinary Time to Feb 16; Lent from Ash Wednesday (Feb 17); Easter Apr 4; Pentecost May 23 (Ascension transferred to Sunday May 16 in California). **Allocation (brief):
January – early February 8 (E04–E11, two a week) · the drive + New Orleans through Ash Wednesday 6 (E12–E17) · the breakups and the asking 2 (E18–E19) · Lent dates through Easter, official, the champagne reveal, to finals 11 (E20–E30).**
**Skye's whole mix every episode she's in** (sex appeal, intellect, wit/play, unrestrained fun — a real, meaningful relationship, never a fling or a cautionary tale; she's genuinely good for him). **Heat ladder in person resumes past E22b**
(the call was words) — **E03 (S2-a, Mon Jan 11 — moved up by the S2E03 rev. 2 pass)**, E05, E07, E09, E10 each a named step (S2-a…S2-e); **E11 the near-miss**; after any no, the heat continues confined to the limit (brief). **From New Orleans (E12) Skye's extra length transfers to Lena** (brief). **Osei's arc: bracing**
(E05 — after the call; again E11). **The overheard fragments** (E04–E11): "…she'd *decided*, Theo…" / "…over Christmas…" / "…the laptop's in my closet…" / "…don't you dare tell him…" — they stop when he walks in; Jack never infers (they click
in E16). Hale's spring seminar (PHIL 109H, "The Novel as Moral Argument") keeps Jack and Lena designated antagonists. **Kevin and Clare Dominguez** (the sad, sincere attempt, E05–E21; she deserved better and knew it within a month).
**Lena's sideways fights target the antics** (photos, clothes, posting, publicity) — what bothers her is that **it's real**; Jack defends Skye on the substance, which makes it worse (brief). **Politics with Skye** after the fundraiser (brief):
Scott Brown, *Citizens United*, the State of the Union, Stupak, the auto bailout and Lordstown, immigration, Hollywood money — "explain it to me while we walk."


**[SRC bible.md · §23.2 S2E04 · "Everyone Is Looking for You" — NEXT episode · L4074–4074]**

**S2E04 · "Everyone Is Looking for You"** · **Tue Jan 12 – Wed Jan 13, 2010** (tag ≈12:10 a.m. Thu) · Year II weekdays: Tue 1 Sam 1:9–20 / Mark 1:21–28; **Wed 1 Sam 3 ("Here I am") / Ps 40 / Mark 1:29–39 ("Everyone is looking for you")**; St. Hilary. *(Full plan: `outlines/s2e04.md` **rev. 1** — pre-production; S2E02 published/unplayed; S2E03 planned (rev. 2) — every gate exhaustive, re-keyed on publication. Title replaces the working "Officially," which S2E03's sign spent. Theo rushes **Sigma Rho** (Brooks; the raw onion; the smoker; Wednesday's bid). **9:10 the mailboxes:** Lena civil ("It was lovely."); she asks how his break was and he gives her the whole week **but the last night** (Lena's asymmetry beat); Laney silent. **1:15 the soccer-stadium lot: Skye teaches him stick — a kiss a stall**; Corinne's "sighting we didn't manage"; **Conan's statement — a promise vs. a time slot** ("Sunday wasn't to a network"). **2:31 the radio: the quake** (1:53 PST; period Trap 1 — no Haiti anything earlier); `s2e4_news`. **5:40 the Fishbowl:** CNN; Kevin's crush syndrome; **Theo to Kevin, neither looking: "How long can somebody last under there?"** (🔒); Margot → Partners In Health. **8:00 St. Brendan's: Lena leads the decade; then the hall — Lena with a clipboard, triage, orders in a whisper (🔒 Celeste's; unglossed)**; Jack on the Wednesday table with Becca; "Who else can you bring?" — [candor] "Skye wants to help." / **"Then bring her."**; Osei redialing a Korle-Bu classmate in Port-au-Prince (Dr. Yaw Boateng — fictional) and "I asked the bishop if he could spare an old doctor. He said he couldn't spare a young priest." **The lounge TV says *trapped*; Lena leaves the room; Julian goes after her** — Jack follows to the door (the back steps: Julian not talking, the cardigan, a nod to Jack) or stays and folds (Julian: "She's okay. She just needed air.") — **Julian's gift, her grief, seen** (`saw_julian_grief`). **11:40 the Fishbowl computer: the Facebook group *In Memory of Nora Calloway*** — the jacket in daylight; members Julian, Priya, Lena; [join] a News Feed story (🔒 Theo sees it later). **Wed 12:10:** Mass for Archbishop Miot [V]; Osei: "Yaw is alive. He's operating in a parking lot." **1:00–5:00 the Newman table on the Commons Lawn under Ferrer Tower: Jack beside Becca — her grandmother's Sunshine Mine rosary told as a sweet fact while the rescue footage plays (§10.4; Jack hears the rest)**; **Skye turns up and borrows Becca's apron** (Becca has never seen TV); **Cassidy says your name for twenty dollars** — a fan's TwitPic with Jack in the back (🔒 S2E08's gossip item); Clare Dominguez's violin, Kevin's water; Theo and the Sigma Rho brothers; **Skye: "Where was God at 4:53?"** — the argument the friends watch (Becca starts "You two sound just like —" and stops); **2:55 Laney's first sighting, verbatim (#89), said to Lena in Jack's hearing — "Oh my God, he found a California you. Excuse me, while I go be a bitch on your behalf."** — Lena's answer an action; Laney does it (funny; the hidden laugh — 🔒 the begrudging like); **Skye meets Lena** (`s2e4_intro`; "You're Lena."; "Yes, ma'am."; one look too long — 🔒 clocked, gracious, never fooled; she never asks Jack); **the alliance mirror on one lawn** (Laney sweet to Julian, poison to Skye; Theo polite to Skye, rude to Julian — "Songs for Haiti. How many are about her?" "One."; Jack says nothing — he stops Theo in S2E07); Julian's **Songs for Haiti**, Café Bastille, Fri Jan 15. **Evening:** Corinne wants a statement about the fan pictures ("There's a boy at the back of every one." "He was holding the cash box.") — **Skye refuses** (→ her first post, E05); **the fork — St. Brendan's open late (her first candle; the Nora plate, unglossed) or the Del Playa porch**; she asks him to say one for them "from me" and says *Amen* late; she asks who everybody at the table was and he names all but one (Skye's asymmetry beat); **"Saturday."** — her want said out loud, the rain check (**no intimate scene — the ladder rests at S2-a; S2-b E05**). **10:35 the front desk: Lena and Priya lock up the cash box — the total; "Skye's envelope was the biggest. Tell her thank you. From Newman."** **10:50 the 4th-floor lounge: emergency meeting #2** — fragment #1 through the door (Laney: "…the laptop's in my closet, Theodore…"); the whiteboard spun to Margot's old USEFUL IDIOTS tally; Kevin "brought here"; Margot "for science"; Theo's date-auction cover. **Tag:** Theo's bid card; Jack relays the thanks to Skye (`s2e4_relay` — by name / "newman" / "the clipboard"); the can on the shelf where the bag was. No picture rung (`skye_pics` 8). **No Jack/Lena two-hander** (the cold war's first week — S2E07's van carries the next).
