## 5.3 The romance roadmap (bible §24) — current state


**[SRC bible.md · §24 The Romance Roadmap — 24.1 Shape (S1 and the winter break/S2 bullets) · L4524–4542]**

\## 24. THE ROMANCE ROADMAP — restraint is the fire

*(Re-planned after S1E11 for the player's MAJOR RE-PLAN, exact sequence and long-range timeline. Superseded plans — the S3E22
first kiss, Gideon, the S4E03 reveal, Andrew, the S7E09 Ephesians, the AD wedding — are retired; see the revision log.)*

\### 24.1 Shape
- **S1 (Fall 2009):** rivals → friends → the two people who stay. She's taken; he's honorable; the heat is all proximity and
  argument. Restraint scenes: E10 (the Brick at dawn), **E19** (the laundry room on St. Nicholas night — *her* fidelity, Jack free; moved from E20 by the S1E19 pass). **No kiss.** The
  timing tragedy runs twice, mirrored: **Wes ends it (E13) and Becca is made official two days later (E14)** — Jack won't be the rebound (his decision; Lena reads it as him
choosing Becca — the hurt chain);
  then **Lena is with Julian (E15) by the time Jack is free (E15–E16)**. **Julian's song (E20) sends Jack into Skye until the finale.** Finale (E22): the empty-campus walk —
  every place from the semester; both nearly say it (brief, E19–E22 design round). Every reason for hope; a real sting.
- **Winter break and S2, January–February 2010: apart in other arms.** Jack + Skye (the fire without the faith; the darkroom
  near-miss); Lena + Julian (faith-respect without fire — and kept, empty, out of fury at Skye: she does exactly what Jack did with
  Becca). Jealousy as POV, both ways. **Proxy fights #1 (S2E09 — Greene, and the Facebook-status grievance) and #2 (S2E12 — the drive, the worst).** New Orleans (S2E12–E17) breaks it open. *(S2 numbers re-mapped by the S2E01 pass — §23.)*
- **S2, February–May 2010: together.** The honesty without letting go, Mardi Gras night (S2E16); both free within a week (S2E18); "No." — she
  cracks up — "Do it properly." (S2E19); three Lenten dates — **the first kiss at the end of date #1, and Lena kisses him (S2E21; brief —
  supersedes the Easter Vigil kiss and "given up kissing for Lent")**; **official at the Easter Vigil (S2E24)** — a yes without an asterisk; the honeymoon weeks;
  the alliance reveal (S2E26); the proxy fights named and the fear answered (S2E28); and, on the last night, the first secret between them — Theo's (S2E30).


**[SRC bible.md · §24.1 — the hurt chain: no retreat after E13, no distance · L4572–4574]**

  clarified):** there is **no retreat after E13 and no distance** — Jack and Lena stay as close as ever and keep getting closer and more
  honest; what keeps them apart is the unspoken hurt each one's relationship choices cause the other (Jack's wait read as choosing Becca →
  Julian; Lena not waiting → Skye; Skye → Lena's fury and Julian kept), never said aloud until New Orleans (S2E10).


**[SRC bible.md · §24.2 Who holds the line, when · §24.3 The dangerous nights · L4584–4611]**

\### 24.2 Who holds the line, when (Lena's resolve is set by writers)
| Period | Jack's resolve tendency | Lena's resolve (writer-set baseline) | Who holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 (to E22) | Mid (party, Theo, smoking) | High (6–8), dips: E11 porch 4, E13 grief written *high* (7: grief makes her careful), E19 ends at 4 (she steps back first — published E19 s21), E20 4–5 (the song sets 4), E21 4 → 6 (the Mañanitas 5; her own confession 6), **E22 6 → 7 (the tower; the blessing) → 6 (the Point; the roof — she holds), ends 6** | **Lena** — and in E20 it is her fidelity that holds |
| Break + S2 Jan–Feb | Low-mid (Skye drains him; the darkroom) | Mid (5–6; Julian steady; the fury drains her) | **Jack** must hold with Skye (the line holds regardless — hard rule) |
| S2 Feb–May | Mid-high (Lent; love; the Vigil) | Mid-high (6–7) | **Both** — the honeymoon is chaste by choice and comic about it |
| S3 | Mid | Mid (6–7; the test told, E18, drops her to 4) | Mostly **Lena**; Jack's held secret costs him |
| S4 | Mid → low (secret; Nonna) | Low-mid (4–5; Nonna's funeral 4) | Trading; never both ≤3 by default |
| SU2 | — | — | Apart |
| S5 | Variable (grief, love, adulthood) | Very low after Wes (3), rising with repair to 5 | **Neither, at times** — the danger windows |
| S6 | Player-driven | 4–6 (engaged; Pre-Cana; confession) | Trading; one night when neither can (window) |
| SU3 → S8 | married | — | — |

\### 24.3 The dangerous nights (only possible when `resolve.jack<=3 && resolve.lena<=3`)
Re-planned for the long-range timeline: **junior year is the height** (brief); sophomore year is restraint *becoming* a thing.
1. **S4E12** (Nonna's funeral, Youngstown, the Costigan basement, after the wake's whiskey). Lena written at 4: **default not
   reachable** — only if Jack's resolve has cratered *and* the writers drop Lena's in-episode.
2. **S5E02** (the night after Wes's funeral, New Orleans; grief and reconciliation in the same forty-eight hours). Lena at 3.
3. **S5E19** (Thanksgiving 2011, Dupre Street, the night Tony gives his blessing). Lena at 3–4.
4. **S6E12** (engaged; spring break; the apartment they've signed for). Lena at 3. The likeliest.
5. **SU3** (the week before the wedding). Lena at 4.
**Hard rule (brief):** every dangerous night is between Jack and Lena. With anyone else (Becca, Skye, anyone) the ceiling is the
near-miss, regardless of resolve (§24.8). If it happens: frank, literary, specific, tender and frightening; then the morning, the
guilt, the confession lines at St. Brendan's on Saturday at 3:30 (both, separately, neither knowing), the rupture and the repair —
as an engaged couple, Pre-Cana in a week. It never destroys them (brief: giving in brings serious guilt, rupture and repair). It
changes them. If it never happens, the wedding night (SU3) is written with the full weight of the wait, and the show is honest that
waiting had costs too.


**[SRC bible.md · §24.6 The proxy fights · 24.7 The secret alliance and its reveal · 24.8 Other-relationship ledger · 24.9 Availability windows · L4654–4729]**

\### 24.6 The proxy fights (reserved; brief)
The organizer is dropped, so the fights now live in **Jack's magnetic-girl period** (official S2E03–S2E11; Lena learns of Skye only in January — the December was secret): Lena is
infuriated by Skye and can't say so (she is with Julian; she went to Julian first; she said "not now" on the roof). A **serious fight that is 100% about
the other person being with someone else, and neither can say it.** Vicious, specific, personal; the subtext obvious to the player and
to Theo, Laney and Margot (who exchange looks and say nothing — alliance discipline); the real reason **never spoken** by either
during the period. Play as argument duels where *winning the stated argument is the wrong thing to win* (a Press/Personal win costs
trust and raises friction; a Concede is the only move that helps, and it doesn't feel like it).
1. **S2E05 (Wed Jan 27, 2010; Hale's spring seminar; the State of the Union that night):** ostensibly Greene's *The End of the Affair*
   (literature as moral test: is Sarah's sanctity real or a writer's alibi for adultery?) — Lena savages the book, Jack defends it; it
   turns into how he's "changing" (the 9 p.m. Mass because he was in the water at dawn — "Since when do you go to the *late* one,
   Costigan?") and whether she's dating a man who agrees with her because she can't stand to lose. Laney: "Sugar, that was not about a
   *book*." Margot: "Well."
2. **S2E09 (Sun Feb 14, 2010 — Valentine's, Bacchus, Laney's birthday; the Broussards' kitchen):** ostensibly the city — the police
   (Danziger; Luc in the room), the Super Bowl as bread and circuses, whether Jack gets to love a place he's known for a week. He calls
   her "a tourist in the class war" in her own kitchen; she calls him "a company man for the company that laid off his father." Frank is
   used as a weapon. Both say the cruelest true thing they know. Laney: "Sugar, that was not about the police."
**The closest it comes (brief; Lena's voice, not verbatim — in fight #1 by default, #2 if the room prefers):** in the middle of the larger fight about
something else, Lena yells some version of **"If we're such *good friends*, how come I had to find out about your girlfriend from a Facebook status, when
we talked every single day for weeks leading up to it?"** — the grievance, never the reason. **Jack has no good answer** (telling her would have meant
saying why it mattered). The fight veers back to its ostensible subject; neither names the real one.
**Rehearsal (S1E10 — not a proxy fight; there was no magnetic partner yet):** the duel `cor` ("On the Heart") at 4 a.m. on the Brick's
eighth floor — the muscle these fights use.
**Payoff:** **S2E19**, once they're together, one of them finally names what the fights were (default Lena: "It was never Greene. It was
never the police."); path may hand the line to Jack. **And for the rest of their lives Lena brings Skye up as a running joke — the
cheapest fallback argument she owns** (brief; #80).

\### 24.7 The secret alliance and its reveal (brief)
Theo and Laney, mirrored, from S1E07: **Laney** openly rude to all Jack's girls (Becca; Skye — whom she privately likes), privately
feeding Theo; Lena tells her to be nice to all but Skye; **Theo** polite to Jack's girls, privately Team Lena; **Theo** openly rude to
Lena's man after Wes (Julian), Jack mostly letting him and then stopping him (S2E04); **Laney** polite to Julian, privately Team Jack.
**Kevin** objects every season; **Margot** likes Julian. Their maneuvering is visible (seating charts, a borrowed car that won't start,
a party invitation that reaches only one of them, a "lost" phone, a reservation under COSTIGAN) and **never acknowledged aloud**.
**Reveal: S2E17 "Floatopia"** (Sat Apr 17, 2010; the first ensemble setpiece after they're official at the Easter Vigil): champagne on a
raft, the toast, and: "We know." / "Everyone knows." / "People outside of California know." / "It was obvious after five minutes."
(player's lines; keep them verbatim).
**The false dawns (brief running bit; registry #68).** Every time Jack or Lena gets out of a relationship, Theo and Laney are absolutely
pumped — and then deeply bummed. It escalates: **#1 S1E13–E14** (Wes ends it; Martinelli's; then Becca is official — the Martinelli's
goes into a ficus), **#2 S1E15–E16** (Becca ends before Thanksgiving — S1E15; a table for two at the Harbor Inn under COSTIGAN; dead Friday with Julian's soup), **#3 S2E11,
inverted** (they hear only the "No." and hold a wake with the real champagne — and then she cracks up). Paid off at S2E17, when the
champagne is finally for something.
**Never ask Laney for girl advice (registry #69).** S1E09 the poem and the knee; callbacks E16 ("No."), S2E03 ("I've seen your work,
Price"), S2E12 ("Don't."); Laney confirms the sabotage at the reveal: "The knee was *art*, sugar."
**Overtime (E09):** as before — the flat, the SL, "bedbugs," the seating chart, the Madonna Inn, the Newlywed Game, the tin. With **Wes**
neither of them was rude; Theo liked him, which made it worse.

|---|---|---|---|---|

Their first time is with each other — a junior-year danger window (§24.3) or the wedding night (SU3) — as the player's choices make it.

\### 24.9 Availability windows (brief structural rule; registry #70)
Whenever Jack or Lena leaves a relationship while the other is free, they would realistically just get together; neither could start
something new with someone else while the other is openly available. So **every breakup with a "nice" partner coincides with a
concrete reason they don't get together right then.** **The magnetic period is the exception:** while Jack is with Skye, Lena can be
genuinely angry at him, and the anger (plus the proxy fights) keeps her in the empty Julian relationship.

| Breakup / start | When | The other is… | The block | False dawn |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lena / Wes ends | S1E13 (Mon Nov 2, 2009) | Jack: with Becca; lets it be official two days later (S1E14) | **Jack's own decision — he won't be the rebound** (her grief; Wes's "Take care of her"; she wants to be asked; Becca's dignity) — and Lena's misreading of it as choosing Becca (the hurt chain, #81); 🔒 the ring Jack knows | #1 S1E13–E14 (dies at the Harbor Inn table; the Martinelli's into the ficus E14) |
| Lena + Julian begins | S1E15 (Thu Nov 12 Bastille; she tells Jack Sun Nov 15) | Jack: Becca's official boyfriend | Becca (no window; re-plan step 2) | — |
| Jack / Becca ends | **S1E15 (Wed Nov 18; accelerated after S1E13; RE-SPLIT)** | Lena: with Julian since S1E15 (days) | Julian — the timing tragedy run backward | #2 S1E15–E16 (dies Friday, S1E16, with the soup) |
| Jack + Skye meet / secret | S1E16 (Fri night Nov 20–21) → S1E17 (the Starlite, Wed Nov 25, before he flies) → S1E19–E22 (secret, uncommitted; kissed the night they met) | Lena: with Julian; doesn't know | Julian; Jack's hurt that she didn't wait (link 2); Skye's "not before Christmas" | — |
| Jack + Skye begins | S2E03 (Jan 2010, official) | Lena: with Julian; finds out at the Haiti table | Julian; then Lena's fury (the magnetic exception) | — |
| Lena / Julian ends | S2E11 (Sun Feb 21, 2010) | Jack: with Skye, ending it that week by agreement | the New Orleans agreement — each ends their own story first | — |
| Jack / Skye ends | S2E11 (Tue Feb 23) | Lena: free since Sunday | none — the real dawn: the ask (Fri Feb 26), "Do it properly," official at the Vigil (S2E15) | #3 inverted |
| Jack & Lena | S4E21–E22 (the rupture) | — | the lie (she learns Jack knew for a year) | — |

Every future relationship, in any season, gets a row here before it is written. (After S2E11 there are none — brief.)

  blinker out, St. Christopher on the visor.

  as the story reveals it; see Episode log.)

  Sacred Heart.

- Siblings: **Luc**, 24 (NOPD officer); **Tess (Thérèse) Fontenot**, 22 (married to Beau; expecting, due Dec 2009);
  **Remy**, 20 (LSU); **Jude and Josie**, 13 (twins).
- **Mémère — Odile Broussard**, 88 (Tony's mother; Breaux Bridge; speaks Cajun French).
- Lena's late grandmother (maternal) — **Lucille Guidry**, "Grandmère" — the Miraculous Medal was hers.

\### 1.5 Looks (from the brief — use exactly)
- **Jack:** 6'0", black hair that needs cutting, pale skin, blue eyes, nose broken once in a CYO basketball fight, stubble
  by noon; St. Michael medal (confirmation); scar through his **left** eyebrow (story told differently every time);
  battered paperback in his back pocket; smokes Marlboro Reds when stressed or drinking; "quitting." Wears his brother's
  old Carhartt jacket, flannels, a gray Cardinal Mooney hoodie, one navy Goodwill blazer.
- **Lena:** ~5'5", sun-streaked honey-brown waves, golden skin, warm amber-brown eyes; soft and curvy; grandmother's worn
  gold Miraculous Medal on a thin chain (touches it when she's thinking or lying — per the brief); thrifted sundresses with beat-up brown cowboy boots
  (Justins) or Keds; oatmeal/gray cardigan in the fog; sometimes a black lace mantilla at Mass.
- **Theo:** tall, tanned, artfully messy blond hair, green eyes, expensive clothes worn like they're cheap; grandfather's
  vintage Rolex Submariner.
- **Laney:** petite, copper hair, porcelain skin, freckles she covers, a laugh you can hear across a party.
- **Margot:** white-blonde hair cut blunt at the jaw, very pale, thick black-framed glasses, all black, oxblood Doc Martens.
- **Kevin:** compact and muscular, crew cut, athletic quarter-zips, a big easy smile that never quite reaches his eyes
  (sanctioned phrase — once, ever).
- **Fr. Osei:** medium height, thin, graying beard, deep laugh lines, a slight limp he never explains; rolled sleeves;
  wooden rosary on his belt.
- **Prof. Hale:** tall, silver hair swept back too long, linen jacket with wine on the cuff, a once-beautiful face gone
  ruddy and broken-veined; devastating smile; dog: an old deaf greyhound named Bishop Berkeley.
- **Julian:** medium height, clean-cut, warm brown skin, short dark hair, calm; guitar calluses; cardigans.

\### 1.6 Phones and tech (2009)
Jack: Motorola RAZR V3, cracked hinge, T9; secondhand Dell Inspiron. · Lena: LG enV2 (keyboard), full-sentence texts. ·
Theo: iPhone 3GS; MacBook Pro (Snow Leopard). · Laney: pink BlackBerry Curve. · Kevin: BlackBerry Bold. · Margot: Nokia
brick. · Hale: doesn't BCC.

\### 1.7 Places
- **CSU Marisol**, Central Coast of California. Classes begin Mon Aug 31, 2009; Labor Day Sept 7; Thanksgiving break
  Nov 25–29; finals Dec 14–18.
- **Delgado Hall** (Anacapa complex, on the Lagoon): 5 floors, co-ed by room. Ground: lobby, front desk, the Fishbowl
  lounge. Basement: laundry. 2nd: 217 Lena & Laney; 204 Rafi. 3rd: 305 Margot. 4th: 412 Jack & Theo; 420 Kevin & Doug;
  401 Priya (RA); 4th-floor lounge with a whiteboard. 5th: roof door "ALARM WILL SOUND" (it won't). East stairwell: best
  cell reception; windows open six inches.
- **Hollis Hall** (1920s Spanish Revival; Philosophy/Classics): Hale's office 314; seminar room 302 (PHIL 9H); section room
  108 (basement).
- **Pacifica Library** ("the Brick"), **the Commons Lawn**, **Ferrer Tower**, **the Grove** (eucalyptus; monarchs in
  winter), **the Lagoon**, **Campus Point**, **the Marine Lab**, **the UCen** (*Marisol Tide* newsroom).
- **Las Olas** (student town west of the Grove): **Seacliff Road** (Greek row: Pi Lambda Chi, Kappa Alpha Delta, Sigma Rho,
  Alpha Tau Kappa; then surf rentals); **the Lighthouse** (last house on Seacliff before the Point; back porch faces the
  Point; gap in the back fence; a bluff path); **Del Playa Lane**; **Shipwreck Cove** (bonfire beach; wooden stairs; old
  schooner ribs).
- **Marisol Point**: headland, ~70-ft bluff, the decommissioned Point Marisol Light (1890s; dark since 1967), a low stone
  wall, a small dirt lot; a white wooden memorial cross at the wall.
- **St. Brendan the Navigator Newman Center** (across Ocean Road, edge of the Grove): 1962 A-frame, ceiling like an
  overturned ship's hull, blue glass, driftwood crucifix, Marian shrine with votive rack and a small brass memorial plate;
  courtyard with an olive tree and a broken sprinkler. Masses: Sat 5:00 p.m.; Sun 9:30, 11:30, 5:00, 9:00 p.m.; weekdays
  12:10 p.m. Confession: Sat 3:30–4:45; Wed 8:00 p.m.
- **Town** (3 mi east on Mission St.): the Anchor (24-hr diner on the harbor wharf; red vinyl, pie case, jukebox); Café
  Bastille (Thursday open mic); the Wharf Rat (dive bar); Sully's (sports bar); Lupe's (taco truck, Seacliff, 10 p.m.–3
  a.m.); Driftwood Books (cats: Boethius, Mrs. Danvers); Giorgio's (pizza); Old Mission Nuestra Señora del Mar; Marisol PD
  on Anacapa Street; Walgreens on Mission Street; the harbor (sea lions).
- Nearest airport: Santa Barbara (SBA), ~55 min south. Amtrak Pacific Surfliner/Coast Starlight on the bluff; a freight
  horn around 2:10 a.m.

\### 1.8 Courses (Fall 2009)
- **PHIL 9H "The Examined Life"** — Prof. Hale; honors seminar; 12 freshmen; Mon/Wed 1:00 p.m., Hollis 302. (Jack, Lena.)
- **PHIL 1 "God, Mind and Morals"** — Prof. Hale; lecture ~400; Thursday 4:00 p.m. discussion section in Hollis 108 led by a
  TA (Jack, Lena).

  spring, maybe** (Lordstown); "Your mother says there's a girl." (`s2e1_frank`). s02 Glenwood 6:40: the blue-lit bathtub Madonna; the neighbors' Santa face-down (the economy); seven stockings; **Becca's cow-stationery card at the front
  of the mantel**; Nonna's splint (cast off); "Carmela Rinaldi. *Tutto.*"; **Theo's gray cashmere throw goes onto Nonna's knees** ("*Questo è di qualcuno ricco*"); "*Viene Danny?*" / "Not this year, Ma."; "*E l'attrice?*" — "She means
  Gina's show" (`s2e1_nonna`). Rosie's buffalo is named Denver. s03 **the Stroh's-sign bar on Market Street** (NFL Network via the owner's Cowboys-fan brother-in-law; the Local 1112 man; **Frank: Canada Dry in a glass**); Saints–
  Cowboys 8:20 ET; Lena from Touro (Tony in the Brees jersey; Mémère's thermos; Luc on break eating Zapp's; Beau; Josie on the record); her LG dies at the half, Remy's phone after; **the 8-Ball: VERY DOUBTFUL**; **Lucille Odile
  Fontenot born ≈10:15 ET in the third quarter while 13–0 — Beau gets no vote** (`knows_lucille`); **Lena is godmother** (`lena_godmother`); Dallas 24–17; "You watched the first one we lost… a pattern. And don't you say

  in the kitchen with five Skye picture icons under it (`s2e1_baby`; [reckless] Rosie's thumb on the down arrow, Gina takes it); "*La bambina della suora*"; 1:04 Skye: the snow globe is snowing.
- **Sun Dec 20 (Advent 4).** s04 St. Brigid's 8:00, **Fr. Dom Bruno** (bad left knee, cough drop, four-minute homily on the Visitation: "go see somebody… Don't make them wait"); Nonna kneels after Communion. s05 **Trumbull** with Ma;

# 7. TIC REGISTRY


*[Packer's note] room.md 'Knowledge Auditor checklist' (L29–40) and the full 'Tic registry' (L41–447: entries #1–#152 and every 'Small repeats to watch' paragraph for S1E02–S2E02), verbatim and complete. The outline's §11 'caps' paragraph (outlines/s2e03.md L1044–1052) gives this episode's per-tic budgets.*


**[SRC room.md · Knowledge Auditor checklist + Tic registry #1–#152 + small-repeats paragraphs (verbatim, in full) · L29–447]**

\## Knowledge Auditor checklist
For every line of dialogue or narration that implies knowledge, ask: **how does this character know this?**
- A character may only reference what they witnessed, were told on-page, or could plausibly learn off-page (and if it's
  off-page, the text must make the route clear: "Laney told me").
- **Banned move:** a character says something they can't know, then plays it off as a joke, guess or intuition
  ("Lucky guess." "I'm psychic." "Call it a hunch."). Cut it or give them a real route.
- Jack's narration knows only what Jack knows. No narrating others' inner states as fact ("she's thinking about…").
  Use observation and inference: "something in her face says…".
- Names: a character doesn't use a name before it's been introduced to them. Jack doesn't know Lena's name until she
  gives it (or someone says it in his hearing).
- Track it in `knowledge.md`: per character, the major facts they know and the episode/scene where they learned it.

\## Tic registry (Line Editor hunts these; add new ones as found)
1. **Wrong word counts.** Narration or dialogue that counts words ("three words," "two syllables," "she said it in
   four words") must be literally correct, or cut. Prefer cutting: counting words is itself a tic.
2. **Knowledge-then-deflection** (see Knowledge Auditor): impossible knowledge covered with "joke/guess."
3. **Pet phrasing repeated across scenes or episodes:** "something shifts," "the air changes," "a beat," "doesn't
   quite reach his eyes" (Kevin's look in the brief is the only sanctioned use), "he realizes he's been holding his
   breath," "the ghost of a smile," "for a long moment," "it's not nothing," "and isn't that something," "Jack files that
   away," "she's not wrong," "he doesn't say…; he says…" constructions more than once an episode.
4. **Overused "jaw tightens," "throat works," "swallows hard," "lets out a breath."** Max once per episode each.
5. **Characters narrating subtext aloud** or therapy-speak in 2009 mouths ("boundaries," "toxic," "triggered," "I hear
   you," "unpack," "valid," "lived experience") unless the character would say it in 2009 (Margot might; mark it).
6. **Everyone is witty in the same register.** Each character's humor must be distinct (see bible voices).
7. **Ending scenes on a one-line paragraph zinger** every time. Vary scene endings.
8. **Anachronisms:** smartphones in common use (the iPhone exists in 2009 but isn't universal; Jack has a flip phone),
   "texting" slang later than 2009, apps, memes, songs or events after the scene's date.
9. **Expert-appraisal similes** (added S1E01): "looks at him the way a [jeweler/customs officer/trainer/mechanic] looks at
   [object]." Max two per episode, and never two in the same act.
10. **"That's not X. That's Y." reversals** (S1E01): max one per episode outside a character's signature line (Theo owns
   this shape; Lena's duel thesis "That's not hypocrisy. That's showing up" is the sanctioned exception for S1E01).
11. **Courtroom imagery** (S1E01): appellate, verdict, evidence, summons, judge, gavel, the record. It is Lena's motif
   (future public defender). Max three uses per episode in prose and dialogue (journal excluded), and only around Lena.
12. **Summing-up closers** (S1E01): "That's all." / "That's the whole X." ending a beat. Max two per episode.
13. **Clue-flag tags** (S1E01): narration pointing at its own plants ("He doesn't know why he notices," "It means nothing to
   him," "He doesn't know what that means. He doesn't let himself try."). Cut them; show the object and move on.
14. **The suppressed laugh** (S1E01): someone laughs against their will, is furious about laughing, hides it in a cup or
   behind a hand. Once per character per episode.
15. **"…for nobody / at nothing" melancholy cadence** (S1E01). Once per episode.
16. **"[Body part] does something [adjective]"** ("his face does something enormous"). Once per episode.
17. **Narrating the theme** (S1E01): the narration explaining Jack's habits ("That's the Costigan way," "It's the family
   trade," "He's good at not going further in"). Show it. One character may diagnose it aloud per season.
18. **Same image twice in one episode** (S1E01): "thick enough to stop a bullet," "stairs in the dark," the lighthouse
   "white and blind," coin/pencil for the cigarette, door/shutter for a face closing. One use per image per episode;
   give each character their own figures (Frank's garage door, Lena's tablecloth).
19. **Mirror-cadence beats** (S1E01): "it reaches her face the same instant it reaches his." Once per episode.
20. **Shared stock phrases flattening voices** (S1E01): "God help him/you/them" belongs to Frank; "designated adult" is
   Kevin's; laminating is Priya's; "sugar" is Laney's, "hon" is Gloria's.
21. **Ranking tags** (S1E02): ", which is worse," "the worst of it," "the dangerous part," "more alarming." Max one per
   episode. Lena's polite-is-terrifying beat doesn't need the tag at all.
22. **Voice-delivery openers** (S1E02): "It comes out [quiet/cold/plain]," "His voice comes out…," "the voice that comes out
   when…." Max one per episode; never open several responses to the same choice this way.
23. **Acting before deciding** (S1E02): "before he can decide not to," "before he's thought," "before the better part of
   him…." Max two per episode.
24. **"Watches her not [X]"** (S1E02): "watches it not help," "watches her not skip it." Max one per episode.
25. **Comic personification similes** (S1E02): "a goose being audited," "a car wash losing an argument," "a mosquito with a
   grievance." Each is good; together they flatten the narration into one register of wit. Max four per episode.
26. **"The way you'd…" similes** (S1E02): the narration's default simile shape. Max three per episode.
27. **Lena's watched exit** (S1E02): "doesn't look back… she does," "and then she isn't," "then the fog/gold has her."
   Once per episode, and never from the second-floor landing again.
28. **Reaction-shot endings** (S1E02): responses ending on "Across the table, Lena…" or "Nobody moves." Max three per
   episode, especially inside a duel.
29. **"…, and is." twist endings** (S1E03): "looks around to see if she's doing it right, and is." Max one per episode.
30. **Margot writes it down as the button** (S1E03): Margot noting, underlining or timing a beat as its ending. Max two per
   episode; once in a while let her not write.
31. **Gesture words "two fingers" and "ceremony/ceremonially"** (S1E03). Max two each per episode (Richard's two-finger tuck
   is the sanctioned one in E03).
32. **Speech addressed to an object or room** (S1E03): "to the oven," "asks the pie," "informs the kitchen," "to the
   windshield." Max three per episode.
33. **"A second longer than [X] needs/has to"** (S1E03; seven uses by E03). Max one per episode.
34. **"Nobody X. Everybody Y." pairs** (S1E03). Max one per episode.
35. **Negation inventories for restraint** (S1E03): "doesn't touch it, doesn't top it," "doesn't add anything." Say it once,
   then show.
36. **"Lets it sit (there)"** (S1E03). Max one per episode.
37. **Negative-action buttons** (S1E04): ending a beat on something a character *doesn't* do ("doesn't write it down,"
   "doesn't say so," "for once, doesn't rate anything"). Max three per episode.
38. **The hollow or aborted laugh** (S1E04): laughs or smiles with nothing in them, or that don't arrive ("the laugh doesn't
   arrive," "the place where a laugh would go," "she laughs, with no laugh in it"). Max two per episode.
39. **"Which from X is Y" verdicts** (S1E04): the narrator grading a minimal gesture as big for that character ("which from
   Priya is a signed permission slip," "on a warmer person would be pride"). Max one per episode.
40. **"…and stay(s) there"** (S1E04; heavy in E01–E02 too): a look, a question, a number or a name that goes somewhere and
   "stays there." Once per episode.
41. **"The [face/look/laugh/pleasure] of a man/somebody who…"** (S1E04; twelve in one draft). Max three per episode.
42. **Hale's scorekeeping as the button** (S1E05): roster marks, whiteboard underlines, a lifted finger, "makes no mark at all"
   ending a duel response. Max two per duel (same rule as Margot's notebook, #30). End duel beats on the combatants.
43. **Give-and-take figures for dialogue** (S1E05; ~ten in one draft): lines "handed back," "banked," "signed for," given back
   "like change," folded "like a receipt," taken "like a letter somebody forwarded," "filed." Max two per episode.
44. **"Like a man / like somebody [verb]-ing" similes** (S1E05): "like a man laying bricks," "like somebody counting what they
   cost," "like a man late for his own wedding." Max two per episode (tic #41 covers "the X of a man who").
45. **"Lets the room / lets that [wait / land / picture it]" timing tags** (S1E05). Max one per episode.
46. **Rereading as an emotion tag** (S1E05): "reads it again," "reads it twice," "then she reads it again." Max one per episode.
47. **"Between them"** (S1E06; 10 in E05, 13 in E06): the default spatial tag for every two-person beat. Max two per episode;
   keep the one that earns it (E06: "a whole person's worth of bench between them").
48. **"He can hear (that) X" interpretation tags** (S1E06; 8–9 an episode since E03): the narration hears the subtext, then names
   it ("It's respect; Jack can hear that much"). Max two per episode; let the dialogue carry it.
49. **Superlative verdicts** (S1E06): "the most tact Jack has ever seen," "the first real question of the night," "the longest
   train he has ever heard." Max two in narration per episode; dialogue gets its own budget of three (Hale, Margot, Lena).
50. **Measuring the Jack–Lena gap** (S1E06): "eight inches of folding-chair gap," "a foot of splintered boards," "a careful foot
   apart." One measured distance per episode; prefer unmeasured.
51. **Speaking while looking away from him** (S1E06): "She doesn't look at him to say it," "looks at the fog instead of at him."
   Max two per episode; never the "doesn't look at him to [verb] it" frame twice.
52. **"In the voice of a [profession]"** (S1E06): "the voice of a funeral director," "a flight attendant doing the exits,"
   "bulletin voice." Max two per episode; profession similes for the cast ("a candidate working a county fair," "like a
   maître d'") count toward it.
53. **"Plain / plainly" as a sincerity tag** (S1E06; 4–7 an episode): "He says it plainly," "plain as a handshake." Max two per
   episode; let the line itself be plain.
54. **"Neither of them [verb]"** (S1E07; 10 in one draft, 11 in E01): the default button for shared restraint ("Neither of them
   says so," "neither of them moves to end it"). Max two per episode; let the scene show it.
55. **Glasses business as a beat marker** (S1E07; ~10 an episode since E01): Osei's reading glasses on/off the cord, Margot's
   frames off / turned over / cleaned / back on, Hale's glasses up on his head, Evan rubbing the marks. One glasses gesture per
   character per episode, and only where it earns it (E07: Margot's glasses off on the roof).
56. **Inch-sized gestures** (S1E07): "raises his thermos an inch," "lifts his coffee an inch," "lowers the phone an inch," "chin
   a quarter inch." Max two per episode. Dale's coffee-inch (E05–E07) is retired.
57. **"Finds (that) he…" discovery tags** (S1E07): "he finds that he would like to," "finds that he means it," "Jack finds he
   doesn't mind." Once per episode.
58. **"Don't know what to do with"** (S1E07): once per season unless it is a deliberate callback (E07: Lena's "I don't know what
   to do with both" on the roof).
59. **Theo's thumbs on the iPhone as a scene button** (S1E08; five in one draft): the alliance tell. Max two per episode; show the
   alliance other ways (he photographs a receipt, asks a time too casually, a phone lit face-up with a text Jack can't read).
60. **"As if / as though" clauses** (S1E08; 32 in one draft, 31 in E04): max twelve per episode, never two in one paragraph.
61. **Time-fillers "(after) a while" and "all the way"** (S1E08; 12 and 13 in one draft): max three each per episode.
62. **"She looks at X. She looks at him."** (S1E08): the look-then-look double beat. Once per episode (Osei's crab double take was
   E08's). "Considers" counts toward it when stacked on the same character in one scene.
63. **"…the trouble" closers** (S1E08; three in one draft): count under #12 summing-up closers. E08 keeps Lena's "It's been a very
   good one. That's the trouble."
64. **One character's figure given to another** (S1E08): Lena's E04 "the way she does everything, all the way" handed to Becca twice.
   Character-defining figures stay with their owner; Becca and Lena never share a simile (the brief depends on the difference).
65. **Reaction adverbs on Becca** (S1E09; "delighted" 35× and "thrilled" 16× across E01–E09): "says Becca, delighted," "thrilled with
   him," "happily," "overjoyed," "glowing." Max two per episode; let her lines carry the joy. Her goodness is the knife, never the tag.
66. **"Jack can't read Lena's face"** (S1E09; four in one draft): "Her face doesn't do anything Jack has a word for," "he couldn't say
   what it's doing," "too quick to name." Once per episode; otherwise give her an action.
67. **Dialogue that opens on "Nobody…"** (S1E09; 45 openers E01–E09): every character's universal-negative aphorism ("Nobody saw it
   coming," "Nobody drives my grandfather's car," "Nobody threads that needle"). Max three per episode, narration included.
68. **"Somebody has to"** (S1E09; 24 uses E01–E09). Max one per episode.
69. **"Whole" as an intensifier** (S1E09; ~20 per path in one draft: "her whole face," "the whole cove," "the whole Grove"). Max six per
   episode. **"For once"**: max one per episode.
70. **Transit codas** (S1E10; about half the scenes in one draft): a scene ending on Jack's logistics to the next place (the bus, the
   elevator, the stairs, "he walks home the long way"). Max three per episode. **"Then the [noun]." sentence fragments** ("Then the 11
   bus." "Then the doors, and the cold."): max five per episode (~17 per path in one draft against 3–10 in E01–E09).
71. **Smell-inventory openers** (S1E10; 3–7 every episode since E01): "[Room] smells like/of [list]" in a scene's first or second
   sentence. Max three per episode, never two scenes in a row; rotate the opener among light, weather and smell (bible §4.2).
72. **"Then, quieter,"** as a dialogue lead-in (S1E10; E02, E04, E05, E06, E08 ×2, E09, E10 ×2). Max one per episode.
73. **Callback glosses** (S1E10): narration identifying where a quoted line came from ("It's Becca's own line, from the stool by the
   stove on Sunday," "her own line, from the laundry room"). Max one per episode; the player binges — trust their memory.
74. **"Chin on his fist" as the listening pose** (S1E11; E02, E05, E06, E07 ×2, E11). Max one per episode.
75. **"Hands / palm flat on [surface]"** as the default steadying gesture (S1E11; 3–5 every episode since E02). Max two per episode.
76. **Indifferent-world codas** (S1E11; 8–14 "goes on / keeps on" per episode since E04): "the sea keeps on," "the stars go on doing
   what they do," "the zebras go on grazing," "keeps coming onto the sand." Max two as scene endings per episode.
77. **"Like a girl at [event]" similes** (S1E11; E01 Jonas Brothers, E03 prizefight, E11 Wimbledon, cut). Once a season.
78. **Ring / velvet-box callbacks** (S1E11): Wes's ring as Jack's motive is narration only, once or twice per episode, each time a new
   image; never the E09/E10 "velvet box on a lace runner" wording again, never aloud, never named to Lena.
79. **"A rumor" for something obscured** (S1E11; E02, E05, E10, E11). Once a season.
80. **Superlatives with a geography tag** ("in America," "in California," "in American letters"; S1E11 had six): count under #49,
   max one per episode outside a character's signature line (Becca's "only honest fruitcake in America" is E11's).
81. **The costume look-over** (S1E12; seven in one draft): a character inspecting Jack (or anyone) head to toe, item by item ("looks him
   over: the feed sack, the rope belt, the hat," "takes them in: hat and hoe, glasses and bird"). Max two per episode; let the line land.
82. **"With great / enormous [noun]" comic gravitas** (S1E12; "with great tenderness," "with great dignity," "with enormous care"). Max two
   per episode. Laney's sunglasses get one adjective per episode, or none.
83. **"Before X can" pre-emption** (S1E12; seven in one draft): "before Jack can ask," "before he has to," "takes another bite before he
   can answer." Max two per episode (Lena's are hers to spend); counts with #23.
84. **"Walks [a distance] with / on it"** as the processing beat (S1E12; four in one duel): "walks three steps with it," "walks half a
   block on it," "walks the next block with it." Once per episode.
85. **The laugh that startles a bystander** (S1E12): Lena laughs out loud and a costumed stranger turns, wakes, sits up. Once per episode.
86. **Osei's delight tags** (S1E12; #65 applied to him): "entirely serene," "to enjoy what comes next," "enjoying every one of them,"
   "never once looked anything but glad." Max two per episode; let his lines carry the joy.
87. **"Which is how X" glosses** (S1E13; seven in one draft, plus E04 ×2, E05 ×2, E07, E09): the narration explaining a gesture and
   buttoning on it ("which is how he applauds," "which is how Gloria ends a conversation," "which is a kind of trust," "which is generous of
   her"). Max one per episode (E13 keeps Lena's "She is funny now, which is how Jack knows how bad it is").
88. **Door-closer exits** (S1E13; E07 had twelve "on its closer"): a door that "sighs shut," "eases shut on its closer," "swings shut on
   its felt" as the button on somebody leaving. Max two per episode; let the person go without the hardware.
89. **Reductive glosses on touch** (S1E13): "It's a shoulder. It's what the step has room for." / "It isn't that kind of holding. It's a
   hand." / "It isn't much to look at. It's what a man… has to give." The #10 "not X, it's Y" shape aimed at intimacy. Once per episode;
   the touch is enough.
90. **Decision recaps** (S1E13): once Jack has decided something on the page, later scenes re-explain it ("he spends it the way he
   decided to at six this morning," "at six this morning he decided not to say anything"). Trust the player who watched him decide; a
   later scene may show the decision acting, never restate it (a character may quote it back, once).
91. **Trailing "which…" commentary clauses** (S1E14; ~14 per path in one draft: "which costs her," "which Jack gathers is rare," "which means she
   was sitting beside it"): the narration glossing an action and stopping there. Max four per episode (#87's "which is how X" counts here too).
92. **"Lightly" as a delivery tag** (S1E14; six in one draft, "Then, lightly," twice — a cousin of #72). Max two per episode; keep it for the line
   where the lightness *is* the hurt.
93. **"Never once" / "has never once seen him do that"** (S1E14; four in one draft, two of them a talker going quiet). Max one per episode.
94. **"Stays where he/she/it is"** (S1E14; four in one draft; 3–4 in E02, E04, E10, E12). Max one per episode.
95. **Becca's "has never [X] in her life"** characterization (S1E14 ×3, E08 ×3, E09 ×2, E13). Max one per episode.
96. **"And goes in" as the exit button** (S1E14 ×4, E10 ×5). Counts with #88 door-closer exits (max two combined per episode).
97. **"Ask me [tomorrow / after X / again in a week]" deferrals** (S1E14 ×3 in draft). Max two per episode, kept for the lines that matter (E14 keeps Theo's "Ask me after Thanksgiving" and Lena's text).
98. **Phone-call silence beats** ("a silence on the line," "a pause in Breaux Bridge," "a silence on Glenwood Avenue"; S1E14 ×4). Max one per episode.
99. **"A sound like…" similes and "inside a minute"** (S1E14 ×3 each). Max one each per episode.
100. **"Doesn't ask" as the all-purpose tact marker** (S1E15; 9 in one draft, ~54 across E01–E15): "and doesn't ask what Jack is doing," "hasn't asked since,"
   "she doesn't ask him what's wrong," usually with a gloss after it. Max two per episode, kept where the not-asking is the point (E15: Lena at the bus shelter, Lena
   about Thanksgiving).
101. **Physical laughter overflow as the button** (S1E15; six or more per path in one draft): Lena grabs the rope to stay on the chair, holds on to the wall, puts her
   forehead on the table, slaps the table so the saint falls over. Max two per episode; otherwise let her top the bit in her own register.
102. **Light-goes-out endings** (S1E15): "the line goes out," the seals "go gray and then go out," a falling star "and out," "the dome light goes off," "the screen goes
   dark." Max two per episode (E15 keeps Delgado's door in s13 and the Ranger's dome light in s28c).
103. **"The voice [X] uses for [Y]" / named voices** (S1E15; a cousin of #52): "the voice she uses to call cattle," "the voice she uses for procedure, the voice from the
   beach cleanup," "his dinner-party voice," "his brightest voice." Max two per episode; never with an appositive second voice.
104. **"Out loud" as an intensifier** (S1E15; 10 in one draft; "laughs out loud" 17× across E01–E15). Max three per episode, kept where the volume is the point (a Glory Be
   at the TV, a deadpan prayer that carries to the pool table).
105. **Waits for more — "There isn't."** (S1E16; 8 "There isn't" buttons and 10 "waits for / waits to see" in one draft, against 0–2 per episode in E01–E15): "He
   waits for the bit. There isn't one." / "She waits to see if there's more. There isn't." Max one per episode (E16 keeps s19's "and she lets there not be").
106. **"X nobody Y" emptiness inventories** (S1E16; rising since E11): "a pool nobody swims in," "two ovens nobody has ever turned on," "a rowing machine and nobody rowing,"
   "a fireplace nobody has lit." Max two per episode; give rich rooms positive details instead (the plastic still on the dials, birch stacked for looks).
107. **Essence glosses — "because she's [Name]" / "because she can't help it" / "because of course she does"** (S1E16 ×4, E15 ×3; a cousin of #72). Max one per episode,
   never as a dialogue lead-in ("And then, because she's Lena:").
108. **"For a second"** (S1E16; 7–8 per path in one draft, three in one scene; E01–E15 ran 1–5). Max two per episode.
109. **The act-drop announced** (S1E16; Skye): negation inventories that tell the reader she has stopped performing — "She doesn't do a voice for it, or a face, or a
   joke," "no bit, no voice of any kind," "not the anchor, not the host," "it isn't any of the laughs she's been doing tonight." Six to eight a path in one draft. Skye's
   real drop is spent at most once an episode (E16: s21a, "And then she drops it"); everywhere else show an action (she caps the Sharpie, takes her thumb off his eyebrow)
   and never grade a laugh or grin against "the earlier ones."
110. **Draft-and-delete beats** (S1E17; five in one draft — s01, s02 ×2, s03a, s11 — after E16 ended on one). Jack types a text and takes it back out "a letter at a
   time." Once an episode, plus the send.
111. **"and means it" / "means every word" / "seems to mean it"** as a sincerity tag (E14 ×6, E15 ×4, E16 ×5, E17 heavier; a cousin of #53). Max two per episode; let the
   line carry the sincerity.
112. **"Then" as the default sentence opener** (S1E17 ≈25–30 per path; E16 higher). Max twelve per episode. Also **"the way [someone] [did]" comparison clauses** (a
   cousin of #26; ≈12 per path in E17) max five, and **"all of it" as an intensifier** once.
113. **The misread tell glossed** — "Jack figures / decides [the wrong reading]" after a fame tell (S1E17 ×3 in two scenes: "He figures she likes the dark end of things,"
   the kid at the counter, "Jack decides it's two in the morning"; a cousin of #13). Once an episode; let the player catch the tell. E17 keeps only s18's.
114. **Narrating Skye's inner state as fact** — "she means every word," "a line she means to understand later," "he watches her decide [X]," "they both know it." Give
   Jack an observation (her face clears; she smiles against his mouth and he can tell). "Watches her decide" once an episode at most.
115. **"The color of [X]" similes** (S1E18; ≈38 across E01–E18 — nickel, oatmeal, dishwater in one draft). Max two per episode; **"dishwater" is retired** (E10, E15,
   E18). Name the thing instead ("The window over the sink is gray").
116. **"Which in this [house / family / wind] means / is" local-custom glosses** (S1E18 ×3: "saved for report cards," "a decorative choice," "two hours and one hinge").
   Max one per episode; it counts under #91. Keep the one with a joke in it.
117. **"Nobody" relative clauses in the narration** (S1E18 ×8: "the one nobody asks Frank anymore," "a coffee nobody asked for," "the one place in the house nobody sleeps
   next to"; a cousin of #106). Max three per episode, and keep them off the scenes where "nobody" is the thread (Gina's "Nobody." oath; Skye's "I liked being nobody to
   you").
118. **"Without looking (up)" as a delivery tag** (S1E19 ×14 in one draft, three of them Kevin's in one scene; E03 ×4). Max three per episode; keep it where the not-looking
   is the point (Kevin's COSTIGAN — LEFT 4Q entry; Lena not looking at Jack after the Personal). Otherwise give the hand something to do or cut the tag.
119. **"It isn't a question." as a narrator's gloss** (S1E19 ×4; a cousin of #10). The line already carries its tone; cut the gloss. A character may say it (Frank's
   "it isn't a question") — the narration may not.
120. **The "has nothing to say / says nothing at all" button** (S1E19 ≈9; a cousin of #37 and #105). Max three per episode; never two in one paragraph (Frank was the father
   who "says nothing at all" twice in s04/s05).
121. **Skye's superlative-verdict formula — "the best/worst X I've ever gotten / that's ever happened to me"** (S1E19 ×8 in Skye's mouth alone; part of #49). Once an episode,
   where it pays off (E19 kept "the best no I've ever gotten" for the PM2 caption). Recast the rest as actions or a flat line ("I'm having that framed").
122. **"At four in the morning" as a figure** (S1E20 ×17 in one draft; E19 ×12, E10 ×13 — "a table read at four in the morning" repeated E19's
   *Questions Only* figure). Name the hour once a scene at most, as a clock time, never as a simile or a mood.
123. **Skye's "since I was eleven / since she was eleven"** (3–4 every episode E16–E20). Once an episode, where it pays (E20 kept s19's "I've been
   looked at for a living since I was eleven"); elsewhere "since I was a kid," or nothing. Never next to her body and money in one clause (Standards).
124. **"In her own voice" as Skye's act-drop tag** (E12, E18, E20 ×3; a cousin of #109/#103). Once an episode, on the drop that matters (E20: "*Jack
   Costigan*," s17). Otherwise just "she says."
125. **"That's the deal" / "deal"** as Jack's and Skye's shared button (S1E20 ×6 across s18a–s19a). Once a path; it is Skye's word (her "better deal
   than my industry," "Good deal") — Jack gets it only as her setup.
126. **The picture-text template** (E19 PM2 → E20 PM3: the phone "low at her hip, taking it," the face "not doing" a character, "looking straight out
   at whoever opens this"). Each picture gets a new camera action, a new specific expression, and how it arrives on a RAZR (stamp-sized, grainy, tilted
   to a light) before any detail. No "isn't doing [X]'s face" negation.
127. **"Considers [object]" as the default comic look** (rising E16→E21: 3, 2, 1, 4, 4, 7 per path; S1E21's draft had Osei and the refrigerator, Becca, the
   sprig, the sprinkler, Theo and the pocket, Laney and the hands, Skye and the fork). Max two per episode (E21 kept Osei's refrigerator and Theo's pocket); otherwise an action, or
   "looks at." Counts toward #62 when stacked on a second look.

\## 11. TICS AND STYLE (this episode's traps)

- **The misreading (s09) — the episode's hardest writing.** Her words exactly; **no adverb on her delivery, no description of her voice, no pause narrated on her end** — the both-ways lives in the bare sentences. **Jack's misreading is the
  episode's one #113 gloss** (≈80 words, his idiom, never "he realizes"); the narration never signals it's wrong; **no later scene restates it** (#90 — not s09a, not s18). **No phone silence here** — he answers too fast (#98 is s15's). **He hangs up first** — no
  "She hangs up first" (#143) in s09. No "Youngstown." The garage is Lena's room; **nothing of Skye in s09** (#64).
- **The pictures (s09a).** No picture re-described (one clause each, a noun phrase: "the trailer mirror," "the bread basket," "the ocean"); counts literal (#1). **The smile is the beat** — give it an action of its own (#136: the episode's grin budget
  is ≤2; this is one); the draft-and-delete is #110's one; Skye's reply registers the softness in her register (a pause typed as *…* once; then a bit) — **not** a #109 drop (Buellton's) and not a superlative (#121).
- **The two-hander (s03–s03c).** Her sounds, never her heart: the cordless chirping, Remy through the wall, the 8-Ball. No "something in her voice." No medal (he can't see it; s09's is told). The promises written straight and warm — no
  wedding word, no narration of what "I do" sounds like, no reaction shot of Jack's body; [Unguarded] — she restarts a question; **no silence**. *The Moviegoer*: terms only. "How's California?" lightly, mid-something, never returned to.
  **"Come back, Youngstown." not glossed.** **The last paragraph of s03c is a fact, not an inference** — no "he wonders," no #58, no #13 clue-flag ("he doesn't know why he notices" — cut).
- **Maria answers (s03).** His mother's half only. "She wants you. I don't know why." comic; the warmth under it unglossed. Nonna: "*La suora?*" and nothing else. The pantry echo of Maria and Danny's call never glossed.
- **The montage (s07a).** One paragraph; "He has called her every night since Sunday." — the narration may not add what he's looking for beyond "where she is." No list of calls longer than three.
- **Becca (s17).** Her goodness is the knife, never the tag (#65 — no "delighted," "thrilled," "glowing"); the rosary and the braid once each (S1E07 small repeats); "has never [X] in her life" none (#95); no ranch similes at her joy (her own lines
  only). **She never mentions Lena.** Jack's guilt is in his hands (a step he stands on, the strap of his bag), never a word.
- **Lena Monday (s18).** **Civil is the weapon and the narration doesn't call it one.** Hale names it ("gone civil") — that's the only gloss; Jack's one line is observation ("she has never once been nice to him"). No "Mr. Costigan" (too
  pointed); "Jack" in a voice for anybody. The king cake is a box on a table. No medal touch (she's not lying; she's not thinking — she's performing; leave the medal alone). No courtroom words beyond Hale's room (#11).
- **The Fishbowl (s19).** **Three registers of wit, distinct (#6):** Skye's improv (she does Corinne; she casts the room), Margot's sociology (Goffman, flat), Theo's silence (his loglines are elsewhere — tonight he's polite and watching).
  Kevin's exit is an action. **Doug's line is the scene's knife and it's said innocently — no one reacts in words; no reaction-shot inventory (#28 — max three a path; this is one); Skye's one action, no gloss; never "considers" (#127).**
  The argument mirrors Saturday's promises — **the narration never bridges (#73)**. McGwire and *The Bachelor* as real public facts only.
- **S2-a (s20).** **Each step named by action, never as a list on the page.** No procedural consent beats; no mechanics; the no to the letter; the heat continues after it. **Never quote the call; never recap it (#90); the [`s2e2_turn`] object/action is
  the only bridge, and it's hers to produce.** Her body described once, literary, specific, not an inventory (#126's spirit). "There he is" ≤2 (hers; s11a [Reckless] and s20 [Candor] are the two — if both fire on one path the Writer drops
  s11a's). No "in her own voice" (#124). **The tag (s20a):** "two of three" stated once; never "one short" (S2E02's); the unfinished prayer unglossed.
- **The double state, held exactly.** Skye scenes end in joy; Lena scenes warm with the knife in; no bridge paragraph; no Lena object in a Skye scene (no tin at Buellton; the Café du Monde can is in the duffel); no Skye object in a Lena scene.
  **Rooms:** Lena — garage, kitchen/pantry, porch; Skye — basement, back bedroom.
- **The team (s14–s14a).** Comic, humiliating, real; **nobody's a villain**. Skye's commentary is **her publicist's voice into a water glass** — not Theo's loglines, not Laney's pageant. Debbie's "baby" ≤3; never the real name. **The folder:
  §15 — one physical object, no memory, no flinch inventory, never §16 beside it.**
- **Buellton (s15).** **Maria's silence is the brief's beat and the episode's one narrated phone silence** — the narration gives only what Jack can tell (she wants to say something; he can't find what); never "she can hear it in his voice" as fact (POV).
  Her drop is the episode's one #109 — no "in her own voice," no act-drop inventory. The call to Maria out of earshot **by staging**. Facebook: a request, a login, Confirm — **no app UI beyond that**; never "Facebook official."

\# PERIOD SHEET: S2E03 "Epiphany" (Fri Jan 1 - Sun Jan 10, 2010; Youngstown, New Orleans, PIT/DEN/SBA, Santa Barbara, Los Angeles, Buellton)

Read it first. This sheet is authoritative for **Jan 4-10** and corrects/overrides anything below where they differ.

\## 0. DATES (from bible 23.1, re-mapped)
- **S2E03 = Fri Jan 1 - Sun Jan 10, 2010.** Days: Jan 1 Fri, 2 Sat, 3 Sun, 4 Mon, 5 Tue, 6 Wed, 7 Thu, 8 Fri, 9 Sat, 10 Sun. Classes begin **Mon Jan 11** (S2E04).
- Anchor beats: Sat Jan 2 phone two-hander; Sun Jan 3 Lucille's baptism / Epiphany; Mon Jan 4 the album; Danny's letter (week of Jan 4-8); **Sat Jan 9 PIT-DEN-SBA, OFFICIALLY sign**; **Sun Jan 10 the team lunch, Buellton gas station, status change, Lena sees it that night.**
- **STALE BIBLE TEXT, ignore:** the bible's Saints schedule line (~4020) places "S2E03 Sat Jan 16 Arizona W 45-14" and "S2E04 Sun Jan 24 Minnesota." Under the current map the Arizona game (Sat Jan 16) falls in **S2E04**; the Saints have the Jan 9-10 wild-card weekend OFF (no game inside S2E03). Also bible text calling Jan 13 the Newman Haiti table is stale (re-mapped to S2E04+; and see Trap 8).

\## 1. TRAPS (read first)
1. **The Saints do not play in this episode after Jan 3.** #1 seed, first-round bye [V: Wikipedia playoff page]. The Sun Jan 10 wild-card result decides their opponent: **Cardinals beat Packers 51-45 in OT at Glendale** [V], so the Saints host **Arizona** Sat Jan 16 (after window). Fox, ~4:40 p.m. ET kickoff (recalled); OT means it ends ~8 p.m. ET / 5 p.m. PT (UNVERIFIED times). A radio in the Saab or a Buellton station TV could carry it; do not assume which way the player's Jan 10 evening runs on sports unless a beat needs it.
2. **Wild-card scores [V]:** Sat Jan 9 **Jets 24, Bengals 14** (at Cincinnati); **Cowboys 34, Eagles 14** (Cowboys Stadium; Dallas, the team that handed the Saints their first loss, wins). Sun Jan 10 **Ravens 33, Patriots 14** (Foxborough, 1 p.m. ET CBS); **Cardinals 51, Packers 45 OT** (record-setting; Rodgers' first playoff start). Steelers (9-7) and Browns (5-11) are home; **Pittsburgh and Cleveland have no playoff stake.** Ohio: Bengals playing at home Sat is the Ohio-adjacent story Frank would hate-watch Jan 9 (UNVERIFIED if Frank watches; he is driving to PIT).
3. **MANGINI IS NOT FIRED.** Browns' Holmgren said Jan 7, 2010 he would **retain Mangini and the whole staff** [V]; Mangini was fired Jan 3, 2011 (a year later). **Jan 10: Browns hire Tom Heckert (ex-Eagles GM) as general manager** [V]. Frank can grumble about Holmgren/"Mangini's still here"; never "fired."
4. **BCS title: Alabama 37, Texas 21, Thu Jan 7, Rose Bowl** [V]; Colt McCoy hurt early (Garrett Gilbert in); Ingram 116 yds, 2 TD [V]. A Thursday-night TV item (Jack in a basement, Frank on the couch). Ohio State beat Oregon Jan 1 (see s2e02 sheet).
5. **Jobs report Fri Jan 8 [V]: payrolls -85,000 in December, unemployment 10.0%**; 2009 lost ~4.2 million jobs; Nov revised to +4,000 (first gain in two years) [V BLS]. Youngstown-flavored dinner-table news (Frank, the union, "Valley"). Do not give a Youngstown-specific rate.
6. **Facebook relationship status needs the OTHER person to confirm.** In 2009 "In a Relationship with [Name]" posted as a request to the named person; until they accept, it displays as pending ("Jack Costigan has not yet confirmed" style), and the News Feed story posts on confirmation (recalled strong; exact UI wording UNVERIFIED). **Beat consequence:** the Buellton status change is Skye's move; Lena sees it that night only if (a) Jack confirms from his phone/laptop, or (b) she sees Skye's profile/mutual friend activity. Jack on a RAZR cannot easily confirm in a gas station: Facebook Mobile (m.facebook.com / touch) exists on BlackBerry/iPhone/feature phones, clunky on a RAZR (UNVERIFIED). The room must decide who confirms and where; the cleanest period-true shape is Skye posts at the gas station, Jack confirms later at a computer, and the News Feed posts then. Also "Sent a request" can be seen by mutual friends only after confirmation.
7. **Epiphany Sunday is Jan 3 (US transfer) but the proper Epiphany is Wed Jan 6.** Befana and the Three Kings custom (Jan 5 eve / Jan 6) fall on Tue-Wed in the window; the Magi move to the crib on Jan 3 at Mass. Italian homes keep the tree to Jan 6; **the Baptism of the Lord (Sun Jan 10) ends the Christmas season** [V: Vatican homily Jan 10, 2010]. A Nonna-dismantles-the-crib scene would be Jan 6-10, tree/lights down by the weekend (Frank's blue lights).
8. **Haiti is NOT in this window.** The quake is **Tue Jan 12** (S2E04 side, after Jan 10). No Haiti table, no Haiti fundraising, no "Newman Haiti" imagery on Jan 1-10, even as foreshadowing.
9. **Do NOT narrate beyond Jan 10:** Jan 11 McGwire steroid admission and classes begin; Jan 12 Haiti quake; Jan 15 annular solar eclipse (Africa/Asia); Jan 17 Golden Globes; Jan 19 Massachusetts Senate special (Brown); Jan 21 Citizens United; Jan 27 iPad. SoCal storms Jan 17-22.

Basil & Gregory Nazianzen (memorial). **Sat Jan 2 vigil Mass for Epiphany** is normal Saturday-evening anticipation (UNVERIFIED parish times) - Lucille's baptism could be Sunday Mass or Sunday afternoon at Holy Rosary (UNVERIFIED local practice; New Orleans parishes often baptize after Mass or in the early afternoon).

Andre Bessette (US optional memorial; liturgical day of the Epiphany in the Italian calendar - but in the US, Jan 6 is an ordinary Christmas-season weekday); Thu Jan 7: St. Raymond of Penyafort (optional); Fri Jan 8 / Sat Jan 9: Christmas-weekday.** (recalled strong for the saints; weekday Scripture UNVERIFIED: the 1 John series continues through Jan 9, Gospels Matt 4:12-17, 23-25 / Mark 6:34-44 / Mark 6:45-52 / Luke 4:14-22 / Luke 5:12-16 / John 3:22-30 across Jan 4-9 - mark UNVERIFIED if quoted).

First reading Isa 42:1-4, 6-7 or 40:1-5, 9-11; second Acts 10:34-38 or Titus 2:11-14; 3:4-7; Ps 29 or 104 [V SLU list]. **Benedict XVI baptized infants in the Sistine Chapel that morning** [V Vatican homily Jan 10, 2010] - a possible TV/news footnote for a baptism-conscious family. Christmas season ends; Ordinary Time resumes Mon Jan 11.
- **Lucille's baptism (Sun Jan 3):** the Church asks that a godparent be a confirmed, practicing Catholic - Lena (baptized Catholic, Holy Rosary) qualifies; a Protestant/other would be a "Christian witness." Julian at the table is plausible; his role at the rite is the show's call.

\## 9. Notes to the room
1. **Two Jan 3 results play twice:** Saints lose (Lena's "They're resting") and the Browns/Steelers win; Frank's mood Jan 3 is good, Jan 4-8 is quiet (no football for his teams), Jan 9-10 is wild-card hangover on TV.
2. **Cardinals-Packers goes long:** it keeps Lena, Laney, Julian (and Saints fans) glued Sunday evening; the status posting that night competes with it. A good source of dramatic irony: the Saints' opponent is decided as Jack's life changes.
3. **The status-confirmation mechanic (Trap 6)** is the most consequential period fact for Jan 10. Pick: Skye posts the request at Buellton; Jack must accept; the feed story posts when he does. Do not use "Facebook Official" as a phrase (it entered usage ~2010-11, UNVERIFIED; "Facebook official" is borderline anachronism by Jan 2010).
4. **Haiti-free zone** (Trap 8): the first Newman Haiti table cannot be earlier than the week of Jan 18.
5. **UNVERIFIED items that must not carry a beat:** weekday Mass readings Jan 4-9; Youngstown day-level weather Jan 2-9 (only Jan 1 and Jan 10 are [V]); PIT/DEN/SBA flight times and weather; baptism time at Holy Rosary; mail transit times; dorm early-return policy; Facebook mobile feature set; King Cake dates beyond "starts Jan 6."
